- section_id: 108
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This space is where cards are placed when they are banished by a spell or effect.

### [official-rule] 108.6.c - Represents cards that have been removed from play in a more difficult-to-recover way, o...

- parent_rule: 108.6
- section_id: 108
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Represents cards that have been removed from play in a more difficult-to-recover way, or a temporary space to hold cards while effects are being processed.

### [official-rule] 108.6.d - The cards in the banishment are unordered. Their sequence does not matter, and they may...

- parent_rule: 108.6
- section_id: 108
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The cards in the banishment are unordered. Their sequence does not matter, and they may be reorganized.

### [official-rule] 108.6.e - The cards in any player's Banishment are Public Information. See rule 128. Privacy for...

- parent_rule: 108.6
- section_id: 108
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The cards in any player's Banishment are Public Information. See rule 128. Privacy for more information.

### [official-rule] 108.7 - The Hand

- parent_rule: 108
- section_id: 108
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Hand

### [official-rule] 108.7.a - Each player has their own Hand.

- parent_rule: 108.7
- section_id: 108
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Each player has their own Hand.

### [official-rule] 108.7.b - This is the location cards go when Drawn.

- parent_rule: 108.7
- section_id: 108
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is the location cards go when Drawn.

### [official-rule] 108.7.c - The cards in a player's hand are Private Information.

- parent_rule: 108.7
- section_id: 108
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The cards in a player's hand are Private Information.

### [official-rule] 108.7.d - The number of cards in a player's hand is Public Information.

- parent_rule: 108.7
- section_id: 108
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The number of cards in a player's hand is Public Information.

### [official-rule] 108.7.e - The hand can be targeted as a zone, and cards there can be affected by spells and effec...

- parent_rule: 108.7
- section_id: 108
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The hand can be targeted as a zone, and cards there can be affected by spells and effects when specified.

### [official-rule] 109 - All Game Objects in the collective Play Areas are Public Information.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 109
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> All Game Objects in the collective Play Areas are Public Information.

### [official-rule] 109.1 - Any player may view, or be provided with the details of the face-up information printed...

- parent_rule: 109
- section_id: 109
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Any player may view, or be provided with the details of the face-up information printed on the cards in the Play Area.

### [official-rule] 109.2 - The State of all Game Objects in the Play Area is also Public Information. Examples: If...

- parent_rule: 109
- section_id: 109
- page: 5
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The State of all Game Objects in the Play Area is also Public Information. Examples: If a Unit is Buffed If a Gear is Exhausted Etc.

### [official-rule] 110 - Whenever a Game Object changes zones to or from a Non-Board Zone, all Temporary Modific...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 110
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Whenever a Game Object changes zones to or from a Non-Board Zone, all Temporary Modifications of all kinds cease to be tracked on it in all capacities. Examples: Damage is cleared. Buffs are removed. Granted Keywords are no longer granted.

### [official-rule] 111 - Setup Process

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 111
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Setup Process

### [official-rule] 112 - Each player separates their Champion Legend and places it in the Legend Zone.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 112
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Each player separates their Champion Legend and places it in the Legend Zone.

### [official-rule] 113 - Each player separates their Chosen Champion and places it in the Champion Zone.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 113
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Each player separates their Chosen Champion and places it in the Champion Zone.

### [official-rule] 114 - Each player sets aside their Battlefields.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 114
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Each player sets aside their Battlefields.

### [official-rule] 114.1 - Your Mode of Play will dictate how these are used and placed into the Battlefield Zone....

- parent_rule: 114
- section_id: 114
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Your Mode of Play will dictate how these are used and placed into the Battlefield Zone. See rule 476. Modes of Play for more information.

### [official-rule] 115 - Each player shuffles their decks, separately, then places them into their respective Zo...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 115
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Each player shuffles their decks, separately, then places them into their respective Zones.

### [official-rule] 115.1 - The Main Deck is placed in the Main Deck Zone.

- parent_rule: 115
- section_id: 115
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Main Deck is placed in the Main Deck Zone.

### [official-rule] 115.2 - The Rune Deck is placed in the Rune Deck Zone.

- parent_rule: 115
- section_id: 115
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Rune Deck is placed in the Rune Deck Zone.

### [official-rule] 116 - Determine Turn Order using any fair random method agreed on by all players.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 116
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Determine Turn Order using any fair random method agreed on by all players.

### [official-rule] 116.1 - Turn Order is established as a repeating set of the players.

- parent_rule: 116
- section_id: 116
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Turn Order is established as a repeating set of the players.

### [official-rule] 116.1.a - The Mode of Play will specify how to determine a First Player.

- parent_rule: 116.1
- section_id: 116
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Mode of Play will specify how to determine a First Player.

### [official-rule] 116.1.b - If not otherwise specified, Seating then determines the sequence, play proceeding clock...

- parent_rule: 116.1
- section_id: 116
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If not otherwise specified, Seating then determines the sequence, play proceeding clockwise from the First Player.

### [official-rule] 116.1.b.1 - The First Player is the player who becomes the Turn Player first.

- parent_rule: 116.1.b
- section_id: 116
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The First Player is the player who becomes the Turn Player first.

### [official-rule] 116.1.c - The Turn Order generates a looping queue of turns, starting with the First Player and r...

- parent_rule: 116.1
- section_id: 116
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Turn Order generates a looping queue of turns, starting with the First Player and repeating until the game ends.

### [official-rule] 116.2 - See the selected Mode of Play for any adjustments to each player's first turn. See rule...

- parent_rule: 116
- section_id: 116
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> See the selected Mode of Play for any adjustments to each player's first turn. See rule 476. Modes of Play for more information.

### [official-rule] 117 - Players each draw 4.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 117
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players each draw 4.

### [official-rule] 118 - In turn order, players perform their Mulligan.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 118
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In turn order, players perform their Mulligan.

### [official-rule] 118.1 - A player may choose up to two cards in their hand. They set those cards aside.

- parent_rule: 118
- section_id: 118
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A player may choose up to two cards in their hand. They set those cards aside.

### [official-rule] 118.2 - Then, that player draws as many cards as they set aside.

- parent_rule: 118
- section_id: 118
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Then, that player draws as many cards as they set aside.

### [official-rule] 118.3 - Finally, that player Recycles the cards that were set aside. See rule 416. Recycle for...

- parent_rule: 118
- section_id: 118
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Finally, that player Recycles the cards that were set aside. See rule 416. Recycle for more information.

### [official-rule] 119 - Begin play with the First Player taking their turn.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 119
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Begin play with the First Player taking their turn.

### [official-rule] 120 - Game Objects

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 120
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Game Objects

### [official-rule] 121 - A Game Object is any game piece that can produce one or more Game Effects or grant prer...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 121
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Game Object is any game piece that can produce one or more Game Effects or grant prerequisites for players to take Game Actions.

### [official-rule] 122 - A Game Object does not include nor preclude any inherent properties beyond the ability...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 122
- page: 6
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Game Object does not include nor preclude any inherent properties beyond the ability to produce, or act as the prerequisite for, Game Effects and Game Actions.

### [official-rule] 123 - A Game Object can be a literal object or a logical object.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 123
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Game Object can be a literal object or a logical object.

### [official-rule] 124 - Game Objects include, but are not limited to, the following: Main Deck cards in any zon...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 124
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Game Objects include, but are not limited to, the following: Main Deck cards in any zone Runes in any zone Legends Battlefields Tokens in any zone Abilities of any type on the chain Buffs and other status markers

### [official-rule] 125 - Cards

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 125
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Cards

### [official-rule] 126 - Riftbound games are composed of players playing the cards in their respective decks.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 126
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Riftbound games are composed of players playing the cards in their respective decks.

### [official-rule] 127 - Ownership

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 127
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Ownership

### [official-rule] 127.1 - Ownership

- parent_rule: 127
- section_id: 127
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> For gameplay purposes, a card's Owner is the player who brought it into the game, either as their Champion Legend, one of their Battlefields, or part of their Main Deck or Rune Deck. The legal owner of a card doesn't matter during gameplay. Example: Bo didn't bring a deck, so Alice loans him a deck. During the game, Bo is considered the owner of those cards, even though legally they belong to Alice.

### [official-rule] 128 - Privacy

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 128
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Privacy

### [official-rule] 128.1 - Privacy

- parent_rule: 128
- section_id: 128
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A card will always exist in one of the many Zones described during Setup.

### [official-rule] 128.2 - Privacy

- parent_rule: 128
- section_id: 128
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A card will have a different level of Privacy based on where it resides at a given time

### [official-rule] 128.3 - Privacy

- parent_rule: 128
- section_id: 128
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Secret: This level of Privacy indicates that no player may read or look at the face of the card.

### [official-rule] 128.4 - Privacy

- parent_rule: 128
- section_id: 128
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Private: This level of Privacy indicates that only the controller of a card on the board or the owner of a card in any other zone may read or look at the face of the card. Example: Cards in a player's hand are owned by that player. Only that player may read or look at their faces. Example: If a player controls a facedown card at a battlefield, that player and only that player may read or look at that card's face, regardless of who owns that card.

### [official-rule] 128.5 - Privacy

- parent_rule: 128
- section_id: 128
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Public: This level of Privacy indicates that any player may read or look at the face of the card.

### [official-rule] 129 - Back Side

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 129
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Back Side

### [official-rule] 129.1 - Back Side

- parent_rule: 129
- section_id: 129
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The back side of a card is the side with the Riftbound logo.

### [official-rule] 129.2 - Back Side

- parent_rule: 129
- section_id: 129
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> There is a unique pattern of back side for each of the three categories of card: Main Deck cards, Rune Deck cards, and Battlefields and Champion Legends.

### [official-rule] 129.3 - Back Side

- parent_rule: 129
- section_id: 129
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The back side of a card is presented to conceal information for Private and Secret information cards. For example, cards in the Main Deck and cards in hand have their back side presented.

### [official-rule] 129.4 - Back Side

- parent_rule: 129
- section_id: 129
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The term facedown is used to describe the state during regular play of having a card's back side presented on the Board. A facedown card’s front side is considered Private Information.

### [official-rule] 130 - Front Side

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 130
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Front Side

### [official-rule] 130.1 - Front Side

- parent_rule: 130
- section_id: 130
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The front side of a card is the opposite of its back.

### [official-rule] 130.2 - Front Side

- parent_rule: 130
- section_id: 130
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is also referred to as a front face.

### [official-rule] 130.3 - Front Side

- parent_rule: 130
- section_id: 130
- page: 7
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This can be identified in situations where the back is not easily discernible by the presence of the Play Cost in the upper left corner of the card.

### [official-rule] 130.4 - Front Side

- parent_rule: 130
- section_id: 130
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Cards, when played, are played with their front face presented.

### [official-rule] 130.5 - Front Side

- parent_rule: 130
- section_id: 130
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The term face up is used to describe the state during regular play of having a card's front face presented

### [official-rule] 130.6 - Front Side

- parent_rule: 130
- section_id: 130
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Cards are considered to be Public Information while in this state, even if obscured by other cards in a pile. Example: The Trash is Public Information because all cards there have their front face presented even while stacked.

### [official-rule] 131 - Cost

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 131
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Cost

### [official-rule] 131.1 - Cost

- parent_rule: 131
- section_id: 131
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Main Deck cards have a Cost, listed in the upper left corner of the front face of the card. See rule 164. Rune Pools for more information.

### [official-rule] 131.2 - Cost

- parent_rule: 131
- section_id: 131
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Energy Cost

### [official-rule] 131.2.a - Cost

- parent_rule: 131.2
- section_id: 131
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The numeral listed in the Cost element in the upper left corner of the card.

### [official-rule] 131.3 - Cost

- parent_rule: 131
- section_id: 131
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Power cost

### [official-rule] 131.3.a - Cost

- parent_rule: 131.3
- section_id: 131
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The symbols, listed vertically, in the Cost element in the upper left corner of the card.

### [official-rule] 131.3.b - Cost

- parent_rule: 131.3
- section_id: 131
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This section may not be present on every card.

### [official-rule] 131.4 - Cost

- parent_rule: 131
- section_id: 131
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Effects that need to determine a card’s cost for any purpose always use its printed cost, even if that cost is altered or ignored as the card is played. Example: Sky Splitter is a spell that costs 8 Energy and says in part “This spell's Energy cost is reduced by the highest Might among units you control.” Lux, Illuminated is a unit that says “When you play a spell that costs 5 or more, give me +3 [M] this turn.” If a player who controls Lux plays Sky Splitter, Lux’s ability will trigger, regardless of how much was actually paid for Sky Splitter.

### [official-rule] 132 - Name

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 132
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Name

### [official-rule] 132.1 - Name

- parent_rule: 132
- section_id: 132
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Each card has a name that identifies it uniquely.

### [official-rule] 132.2 - Name

- parent_rule: 132
- section_id: 132
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is usually located in the middle of the card.

### [official-rule] 132.3 - Name

- parent_rule: 132
- section_id: 132
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Cards that are printed in different languages but represent the same card are considered to have the same name for the purposes of deckbuilding and gameplay. Example: Chemtech Enforcer in English and its counterpart in Chinese are considered the same card, despite the Name element on the card reading differently.

### [official-rule] 132.4 - Name

- parent_rule: 132
- section_id: 132
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Some cards have both a short name and a subtitle. For all purposes, including rules and deckbuilding, such a card’s name is “[Short Name], [Subtitle]”. Example: Kai’Sa, Evolutionary and Kai’Sa, Survivor both have the short name Kai’Sa, but they have different names. You can include 3 of each in your deck under normal deckbuilding rules. If one of them is your Chosen Champion, the other is not.

### [official-rule] 133 - Category

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 133
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Category

### [official-rule] 133.1 - Category

- parent_rule: 133
- section_id: 133
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A card can have one or more Categories and Sub-Categories based on the properties of its front and back sides.

### [official-rule] 133.2 - Category

- parent_rule: 133
- section_id: 133
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Categories and Sub-Categories dictate the behaviors of the card during play.

### [official-rule] 133.3 - Category

- parent_rule: 133
- section_id: 133
- page: 8
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spells and other effects can refer to categories, sub-categories, supertypes, card types, tags, and other characteristics inclusively or exclusively. Example: A "non-unit card" is any card that is not a unit. Example: A "unit" is any game object that is a unit, regardless of any other categories it belongs to.

### [official-rule] 133.4 - Category

- parent_rule: 133
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Main Deck Cards begin the game in the Main Deck or (in the case of a Chosen Champion) the Champion Zone.

### [official-rule] 133.4.a - Category

- parent_rule: 133.4
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Permanents

### [official-rule] 133.4.a.1 - Category

- parent_rule: 133.4.a
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> An umbrella sub-category encompassing Main Deck Game Objects that remain on the board after being played.

### [official-rule] 133.4.a.2 - Category

- parent_rule: 133.4.a
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Unit and Gear are permanent types.

### [official-rule] 133.4.b - Category

- parent_rule: 133.4
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spells

### [official-rule] 133.4.b.1 - Category

- parent_rule: 133.4.b
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A sub-category encompassing Main Deck cards with the spell type, which do not remain on the board after being played.

### [official-rule] 133.5 - Category

- parent_rule: 133
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Rune Deck Cards begin the game in the Rune Deck.

### [official-rule] 133.5.a - Category

- parent_rule: 133.5
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Runes

### [official-rule] 133.5.a.1 - Category

- parent_rule: 133.5.a
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A sub-category encompassing cards with the rune type. These are channeled rather than played. They remain on the board after being channeled, but they are not permanents, as they are not Main Deck cards.

### [official-rule] 133.6 - Category

- parent_rule: 133
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Non-Deck Cards are not part of any deck and begin the game in a zone determined by their type.

### [official-rule] 133.6.a - Category

- parent_rule: 133.6
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Battlefields

### [official-rule] 133.6.a.1 - Category

- parent_rule: 133.6.a
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A sub-category encompassing cards with the battlefield type. They are not played or channeled. They start the game on the board.

### [official-rule] 133.6.b - Category

- parent_rule: 133.6
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Legends

### [official-rule] 133.6.b.1 - Category

- parent_rule: 133.6.b
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A sub-category encompassing cards with the legend type. They are not played or channeled. They start the game in the Legend Zone and cannot leave it during play.

### [official-rule] 133.7 - Category

- parent_rule: 133
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Supertypes are Categories that may apply to game objects of multiple types. They are listed before a card’s type.

### [official-rule] 133.7.a - Category

- parent_rule: 133.7
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Champion is a supertype that applies exclusively to units. It impacts the rules for deckbuilding. See rule 103.2.a. Chosen Champion for more information.

### [official-rule] 133.7.b - Category

- parent_rule: 133.7
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Signature is a supertype that may apply to game objects of any card type. It impacts the rules for deckbuilding. See rule 103.2.d. for more information about Signature cards.

### [official-rule] 133.7.c - Category

- parent_rule: 133.7
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Token is a supertype applied to temporary game objects of any card type. See rule 176. Tokens for more information.

### [official-rule] 133.8 - Category

- parent_rule: 133
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tags are Categories that may apply to game objects of multiple types. They are listed after a card’s type.

### [official-rule] 133.8.a - Category

- parent_rule: 133.8
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tags have no innate rules meaning, but may be referenced by game rules and card effects.

### [official-rule] 133.8.b - Category

- parent_rule: 133.8
- section_id: 133
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tags used to link Legends, Champion Units, and Signature cards are known as Champion Tags.

### [official-rule] 134 - Domain

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 134
- page: 9
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Domain

### [official-rule] 134.1 - Domain

- parent_rule: 134
- section_id: 134
- page: 9-10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Most cards belong to one or more of six Domains, identified by one or more symbols in the lower right corner.

### [official-rule] 134.2 - Domain

- parent_rule: 134
- section_id: 134
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Each Domain has an associated color, a unique symbol, and a shorthand used to represent that symbol in written text.

### [official-rule] 134.2.a - Domain

- parent_rule: 134.2
- section_id: 134
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Fury is associated with the color red and represented by a circular symbol with three projecting points. Its shorthand is [R].

### [official-rule] 134.2.b - Domain

- parent_rule: 134.2
- section_id: 134
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Calm is associated with the color green and represented by a symbol resembling a leaf. Its shorthand is [G].

### [official-rule] 134.2.c - Domain

- parent_rule: 134.2
- section_id: 134
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Mind is associated with the color blue and represented by a symbol resembling a sun and moon. Its shorthand is [B].

### [official-rule] 134.2.d - Domain

- parent_rule: 134.2
- section_id: 134
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Body is associated with the color orange and represented by a blocky diamond-shaped symbol. Its shorthand is [O].

### [official-rule] 134.2.e - Domain

- parent_rule: 134.2
- section_id: 134
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Chaos is associated with the color purple and represented by a hexagonal symbol with swirls emanating from its center. Its shorthand is [P].

### [official-rule] 134.2.f - Domain

- parent_rule: 134.2
- section_id: 134
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Order is associated with the color yellow and represented by an angular winged symbol. Its shorthand is [Y].

### [official-rule] 135 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Rules Text

### [official-rule] 135.1 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> All cards have a section that describes how they affect the game. This section is known as the Rules Text of the card.

### [official-rule] 135.2 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This section may contain:

### [official-rule] 135.2.a - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Abilities

### [official-rule] 135.2.a.1 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.a
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Abilities are discrete pieces of text that describe what a card may or must do. See rule 360. Abilities for more information.

### [official-rule] 135.2.a.2 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.a
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> They may appear on any card.

### [official-rule] 135.2.b - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Instructions

### [official-rule] 135.2.b.1 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.b
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Instructions are a type of text that appears on spells and within abilities.

### [official-rule] 135.2.b.2 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.b
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> They describe the actions to be taken as the spell or ability resolves.

### [official-rule] 135.2.b.3 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.b
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Instructions will self-describe when they are to be executed. If there is no timing described, they will execute during resolution. Example: An instruction says “as you play me, kill a unit as an additional cost.” That instruction will execute as the card is being played. If the card is given Repeat and the Repeat cost is paid, this instruction will not execute because the Repeat execution does not happen until resolution of the spell, after this instruction has executed.

### [official-rule] 135.2.b.4 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.b
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Instructions can usually be recognized by the way they are written. An instruction is written in the imperative mood, using the base form of a verb and omitting the subject unless necessary.

### [official-rule] 135.2.c - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Keywords

### [official-rule] 135.2.c.1 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.c
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Keywords are short words or phrases that represent longer abilities or instructions. See rule 800. Keywords for more information.

### [official-rule] 135.2.c.2 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.c
- section_id: 135
- page: 10
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Keywords may or may not have reminder text.

### [official-rule] 135.2.d - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Reminder Text

### [official-rule] 135.2.d.1 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.d
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Reminder text appears in italics and parentheses.

### [official-rule] 135.2.d.2 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.d
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Its purpose is to summarize relevant rules, including keywords and other rules.

### [official-rule] 135.2.d.3 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.d
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The presence, absence, or exact wording of reminder text has no effect on game function.

### [official-rule] 135.2.e - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Symbols

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.1 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Rules text sometimes contains symbols that express game concepts. Each symbol also has a shorthand used to represent it in written text.

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.2 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The cost of Exhausting a Permanent is represented by the “exhaust symbol,” which resembles a card turning sideways. Its shorthand is [E]. (Some previous rules information has given its shorthand as [T].)

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.3 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Might is represented by the “Might symbol,” which resembles a sword and shield. Its shorthand is [M]. (Some previous rules information has given its shorthand as [S].)

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.4 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Power of a specific Domain is represented by its domain symbol and the corresponding shorthand. See rule 134. Domain for more information.

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.5 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Power of any Domain is represented by a swirling rainbow symbol. Its shorthand is [A].

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.5.a - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e.5
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When required as a cost, [A] can be paid by Power of any Domain.

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.5.b - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e.5
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When Added to a player’s Rune Pool, [A] can be spent to pay a Power cost of any Domain.

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.6 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Power of a domain corresponding to a card’s own Domain is not represented by a symbol, but appears in these rules similarly to other symbols. Its shorthand is [C].

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.6.a - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e.6
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a [C] shorthand is printed on a card as reminder text, it assumes that card’s usual Domain, but it still means “one Power of this card’s Domain.”

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.6.b - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e.6
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A [C] shorthand on a card with no Domain or with multiple Domains is processed as [A] instead.

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.7 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A keyword associated with an ability is indicated by the [>] symbol.

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.7.a - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e.7
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This could be a dependent keyword like [Legion][>] or [Level][>], or it could be a permissive keyword like [Action][>] or [Reaction][>].

### [official-rule] 135.2.e.7.b - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.2.e.7
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Whatever instruction or ability comes after the [>] is modified by the keyword that comes before the [>]. Either it will be inactive, in the case of a dependent keyword, or it will receive the permission granted by the keyword in the case of a permissive keyword.

### [official-rule] 135.3 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Rules text can be blank.

### [official-rule] 135.4 - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A card’s printed Rules Text is Inactive while that card is Attached to another card. See rule 716. Attachment for more information. See rule 720. Inactive for more information.

### [official-rule] 135.4.a - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.4
- section_id: 135
- page: 11
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The presence of text, rules, Keywords, and other effects can still be referenced and detected by other game effects, even while Rules Text is Inactive.

### [official-rule] 135.4.b - Rules Text

- parent_rule: 135.4
- section_id: 135
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Any granted or appended Rules Text on a card is still Active even if that card is Attached to another card.

### [official-rule] 136 - Effect Text

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 136
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Effect Text

### [official-rule] 136.1 - Effect Text

- parent_rule: 136
- section_id: 136
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Some cards have a separate section of text below the Rules Text. This is referred to as the Effect Text.

### [official-rule] 136.2 - Effect Text

- parent_rule: 136
- section_id: 136
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Effect Text can contain additional Abilities.

### [official-rule] 136.2.a - Effect Text

- parent_rule: 136.2
- section_id: 136
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Effect Text can be blank.

### [official-rule] 136.2.b - Effect Text

- parent_rule: 136.2
- section_id: 136
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Effect Text is inactive unless the card with the Effect Text is Attached to another card. See rule 716. Attachment for more information. See rule 720. Inactive for more information.

### [official-rule] 136.2.c - Effect Text

- parent_rule: 136.2
- section_id: 136
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The abilities in the Effect Text section of a card are appended to the Rules Text of the card to which the card with the Effect Text is Attached.

### [official-rule] 137 - Might Bonus

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 137
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Might Bonus

### [official-rule] 137.1 - Might Bonus

- parent_rule: 137
- section_id: 137
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Some cards have a Might Bonus in their lower right corner, expressed as an operator plus an integer.

### [official-rule] 137.2 - Might Bonus

- parent_rule: 137
- section_id: 137
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A card’s Might Bonus can be +0.

### [official-rule] 137.3 - Might Bonus

- parent_rule: 137
- section_id: 137
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A card’s Might Bonus modulates the Might of the card to which the card that has the Might Bonus is Attached. See rule 716. Attachment for more information.

### [official-rule] 137.3.a - Might Bonus

- parent_rule: 137.3
- section_id: 137
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Might Bonus is applied while Attached and stops applying as soon as the card with the Might Bonus is no longer Attached.

### [official-rule] 137.3.b - Might Bonus

- parent_rule: 137.3
- section_id: 137
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the card to which a card with a Might Bonus is Attached has no Might value, the Might Bonus is ignored.

### [official-rule] 138 - Flavor Text

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 138
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Flavor Text

### [official-rule] 138.1 - Flavor Text

- parent_rule: 138
- section_id: 138
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Flavor text is text that appears at the bottom of some cards to provide aesthetic enjoyment. It conveys no gameplay information. Example: The card Void Seeker features a quote from the character Kai'Sa. It does not have the Kai'Sa tag and has no gameplay connection to the Champion unit Kai'Sa.

### [official-rule] 138.2 - Flavor Text

- parent_rule: 138
- section_id: 138
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Flavor text is located at the bottom of the section provided for rules text, usually in a shaded bar. It appears in italics.

### [official-rule] 139 - Illustration

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 139
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Illustration

### [official-rule] 139.1 - Illustration

- parent_rule: 139
- section_id: 139
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Each card has an illustration. It conveys no gameplay information.

### [official-rule] 140 - Units

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 140
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units

### [official-rule] 141 - Unit is:

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 141
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Unit is:

### [official-rule] 141.1 - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141
- section_id: 141
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Game Object

### [official-rule] 141.1.a - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.1
- section_id: 141
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> While on the Board:

### [official-rule] 141.1.a.1 - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.1.a
- section_id: 141
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units are at one of several Locations while on the Board: a Battlefield or their Base.

### [official-rule] 141.1.a.2 - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.1.a
- section_id: 141
- page: 12
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units and their details are Public Information while on the Board.

### [official-rule] 141.1.a.3 - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.1.a
- section_id: 141
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units can be chosen, affected, or manipulated by spells, affects, or game actions that specify Units.

### [official-rule] 141.1.a.4 - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.1.a
- section_id: 141
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units can be Killed. See rule 428. Kill for more information.

### [official-rule] 141.1.b - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.1
- section_id: 141
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> While in the Trash:

### [official-rule] 141.1.b.1 - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.1.b
- section_id: 141
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units are treated as Cards, similar to when in the Hand.

### [official-rule] 141.1.b.2 - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.1.b
- section_id: 141
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> They retain the properties of being a Unit, but are not on the Board and thus cannot take actions or be affected by spells, abilities, or game actions that target Units on the Board.

### [official-rule] 141.1.b.3 - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.1.b
- section_id: 141
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units can be affected by spells and game effects that target Units in the Trash.

### [official-rule] 141.2 - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141
- section_id: 141
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Card Type

### [official-rule] 141.2.a - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.2
- section_id: 141
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is a unique identifier that some spells or abilities will use to restrict what they can choose or affect.

### [official-rule] 141.2.b - Unit is:

- parent_rule: 141.2
- section_id: 141
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The card type is relevant in all zones.

### [official-rule] 142 - Damage is a marked value that is applied to Units.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 142
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage is a marked value that is applied to Units.

### [official-rule] 142.1 - Damage is not a Game Object.

- parent_rule: 142
- section_id: 142
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage is not a Game Object.

### [official-rule] 142.2 - Damage is a value tracked per-Unit.

- parent_rule: 142
- section_id: 142
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage is a value tracked per-Unit.

### [official-rule] 142.3 - Damage tracks how close a Unit is to being Killed. See rule 428. Kill for more informat...

- parent_rule: 142
- section_id: 142
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage tracks how close a Unit is to being Killed. See rule 428. Kill for more information.

### [official-rule] 142.4 - Damage can be Healed. See rule 418. Heal for more information.

- parent_rule: 142
- section_id: 142
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage can be Healed. See rule 418. Heal for more information.

### [official-rule] 143 - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

### [official-rule] 143.1 - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tag: A Unit has zero or more Tags representing one or more champions, regions, factions, or species it belongs to.

### [official-rule] 143.1.a - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143.1
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These have no intrinsic rules or behaviors by themselves.

### [official-rule] 143.1.b - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143.1
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spells, abilities, and game actions can reference these types as part of their execution.

### [official-rule] 143.2 - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Might: The combat statistic of a Unit. Used to determine a Unit's contribution to Combat, as well as when it is Killed by damaging effects.

### [official-rule] 143.2.a - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143.2
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Unit ever has nonzero damage marked on it equalling or exceeding its Might, it is Killed.

### [official-rule] 143.2.b - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143.2
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a unit's Might is ever less than 0, it is treated as 0 when referenced by spells and abilities, and when summing Might to be assigned as damage in the Combat Damage Step. See rule 460. The Combat Damage Step for more information.

### [official-rule] 143.2.b.1 - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143.2.b
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Although the unit’s Might is treated as 0, it is not 0. Effects that calculate Might increases and decreases use the actual value of the unit’s Might.

### [official-rule] 143.3 - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units can have damage marked on them.

### [official-rule] 143.3.a - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143.3
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When spells, abilities, or other game effects deal damage, Units mark that damage on them temporarily. This can be tracked with coins, dice, or other markers, or by memory.

### [official-rule] 143.3.b - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143.3
- section_id: 143
- page: 13
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage is Healed from Units at two specific times:

### [official-rule] 143.3.b.1 - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143.3.b
- section_id: 143
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> At the end of each player's turn.

### [official-rule] 143.3.b.2 - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143.3.b
- section_id: 143
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> During a Combat Cleanup. See rule 461.1. for more information about Combat Cleanups.

### [official-rule] 143.4 - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143
- section_id: 143
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units enter the Board exhausted.

### [official-rule] 143.4.a - Units have multiple Intrinsic Properties unique to them:

- parent_rule: 143.4
- section_id: 143
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This can be altered by Accelerate or similar game effects. See rule 805. Accelerate for more information.

### [official-rule] 144 - Units have the Inherent Ability to perform a Standard Move.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

> Units have the Inherent Ability to perform a Standard Move.

- parent_rule: 144
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action is limited in when it can be performed.

### [official-rule] 144.1.a - This action can be done any time during a player's Main Phase.

- parent_rule: 144.1
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action can be done any time during a player's Main Phase.

### [official-rule] 144.1.b - This action cannot be performed during a Closed State.

- parent_rule: 144.1
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action cannot be performed during a Closed State.

### [official-rule] 144.1.c - This action cannot be performed during a Showdown or Combat.

- parent_rule: 144.1
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action cannot be performed during a Showdown or Combat.

### [official-rule] 144.2 - Exhausting the Unit is the Cost for this action.

- parent_rule: 144
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Exhausting the Unit is the Cost for this action.

### [official-rule] 144.3 - Players may perform multiple Units' standard move simultaneously.

- parent_rule: 144
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may perform multiple Units' standard move simultaneously.

### [official-rule] 144.3.a - When a Move like this is declared by a player, the units' Destination must be the same.

- parent_rule: 144.3
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a Move like this is declared by a player, the units' Destination must be the same.

### [official-rule] 144.3.b - When a Move like this is declared by a player, the Origins do not need to be the same.

- parent_rule: 144.3
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a Move like this is declared by a player, the Origins do not need to be the same.

### [official-rule] 144.3.c - The Costs of Exhausting the Units are also paid Simultaneously.

- parent_rule: 144.3
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Costs of Exhausting the Units are also paid Simultaneously.

### [official-rule] 144.4 - The Destinations where Units can Move to with their Standard Move are restricted:

- parent_rule: 144
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Destinations where Units can Move to with their Standard Move are restricted:

### [official-rule] 144.4.a - Units may move from their Base to a Battlefield.

- parent_rule: 144.4
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

### [official-rule] 144.4.a.1 - Units cannot Move to a Battlefield that already has units from 2 other players present,...

- parent_rule: 144.4.a
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units cannot Move to a Battlefield that already has units from 2 other players present, or where a Combat is ongoing that has 2 other players as participants.

### [official-rule] 144.4.b - Units may move from a Battlefield to their Base.

- parent_rule: 144.4
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units may move from a Battlefield to their Base.

### [official-rule] 144.4.c - Ganking is a unique ability that affects a Unit's Standard Move

- parent_rule: 144.4
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Ganking is a unique ability that affects a Unit's Standard Move

### [official-rule] 144.4.c.1 - Units with Ganking may use their Standard Move to Move from Battlefield to Battlefield....

- parent_rule: 144.4.c
- section_id: 144
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units with Ganking may use their Standard Move to Move from Battlefield to Battlefield. See rule 810. Ganking for more information.

- section_id: 145
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units may have Activated Abilities.

- parent_rule: 145
- section_id: 145
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Activated Abilities are Game Effects that are written as Costs followed by a ":", and then succeeded by an effect. See rule 376. Activated Abilities for more information.

- parent_rule: 145

- page: 14

Official rule text:

> The Activated Ability of Units may be executed at any time during the controlling player's Main Phase during an Open State, and not during a Showdown.

- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This follows the same process as playing a card. See rule 349. Playing Cards for more information.

- parent_rule: 145.2.a
- section_id: 145
- page: 14
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This behaves, once activated, like a spell without an associated card.

- section_id: 148

- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

> While on the Board.

### [official-rule] 148.1.a.1 - Gear are:

- parent_rule: 148.1.a
- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

- parent_rule: 148.1.a
- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

- parent_rule: 148.1.a
- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Gear can be Killed. See rule 428. Kill for more information.

- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

- parent_rule: 148.1.b
- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> They retain the properties of being Gear, but are not on the Board and thus cannot take actions or be affected by spells, abilities, or game actions that affect Gear on the Board.

### [official-rule] 148.1.b.3 - Gear are:

- parent_rule: 148.1.b
- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Gear can be affected by spells and game effects that affect Gear in the Trash.

### [official-rule] 148.2 - Gear are:

- parent_rule: 148
- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Card Type

### [official-rule] 148.2.a - Gear are:

- parent_rule: 148.2
- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is a unique identifier that some spells or abilities will use to restrict what they can choose or affect.

### [official-rule] 148.2.b - Gear are:

- parent_rule: 148.2
- section_id: 148
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The card type is relevant in all zones.

- section_id: 149

- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

- parent_rule: 149
- section_id: 149
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

- section_id: 150
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

- parent_rule: 150
- section_id: 150
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Activated Abilities are Game Effects that are written as Costs followed by a ":", and then succeeded by an effect. See rule 376. Activated Abilities for more information.

- parent_rule: 150
- section_id: 150
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Activated Ability of Gear may be executed at any time during the controlling player's Main Phase during an Open State, and not during a Showdown.

- parent_rule: 150.2
- section_id: 150
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This follows the same process as playing a card. See rule 349. Playing Cards for more information.

- parent_rule: 150.2.a
- section_id: 150
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

> This behaves, once activated, like a spell without an associated card.

### [official-rule] 151 - Gear have a Location.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 151
- page: 15
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Gear have a Location.

### [official-rule] 151.1 - A Gear’s Location is the Base or Battlefield it currently occupies.

- parent_rule: 151
- section_id: 151
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Gear’s Location is the Base or Battlefield it currently occupies.

### [official-rule] 151.2 - Gear cannot normally become located at a Battlefield unless by some special means. Exam...

- parent_rule: 151
- section_id: 151
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Gear cannot normally become located at a Battlefield unless by some special means. Example: Gear are always played to Base. The only exception is a gear played from Facedown, which is played to the Battlefield it was played from. Gear can also become located at a Battlefield if they are attached to a unit who becomes located at a Battlefield. See rule 811. Hidden for more information. See rule 716. Attachment for more information.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 152
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spells

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 153
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spell is a card type.

### [official-rule] 154 - A spell can be played during an Open State outside of Showdowns on its controller's turn.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 154
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A spell can be played during an Open State outside of Showdowns on its controller's turn.

### [official-rule] 155 - A spell is controlled by the player who played it.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 155
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A spell is controlled by the player who played it.

### [official-rule] 156 - A spell creates a game effect according to its instructions and is then placed in the T...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 156
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A spell creates a game effect according to its instructions and is then placed in the Trash of the player who owns it.

### [official-rule] 157 - When a spell is successfully played, a player executes the rules text of the spell. Thi...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 157
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a spell is successfully played, a player executes the rules text of the spell. This is called Resolving the spell.

### [official-rule] 157.1 - Spells have their rules text executed from top to bottom when they are Resolved.

- parent_rule: 157
- section_id: 157
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spells have their rules text executed from top to bottom when they are Resolved.

### [official-rule] 157.2 - If a later part of a spell applies a Replacement Effect that alters earlier parts of th...

- parent_rule: 157
- section_id: 157
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a later part of a spell applies a Replacement Effect that alters earlier parts of the spell, apply those replacement effects as appropriate. Example: A spell says "Choose a unit. Kill it the next time it takes damage this turn." and "[Legion] — Kill it now instead. (Get the effect if you've played another card this turn.)" If the Legion condition is satisfied, the unit is killed immediately and the instruction to kill it the next time it takes damage is ignored, even if the unit remains on the board somehow. See rule 367. Replacement Effects for more information.

### [official-rule] 157.3 - While a spell or ability on the chain is Resolving, no other spells or abilities can be...

- parent_rule: 157
- section_id: 157
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> While a spell or ability on the chain is Resolving, no other spells or abilities can be finalized on the chain or resolved, including triggered abilities or game effects that would occur as a result of the execution of the spell.

### [official-rule] 157.3.a - Finish resolving all effects of a spell before addressing any chain items or Tasks the...

- parent_rule: 157.3
- section_id: 157
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Finish resolving all effects of a spell before addressing any chain items or Tasks the spell may have added or made outstanding through execution.

### [official-rule] 158 - Certain Keywords on spells are not executable rules text, but instead intrinsic propert...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 158
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Certain Keywords on spells are not executable rules text, but instead intrinsic properties of the spell.

### [official-rule] 158.1 - These determine inherent properties and behaviors of the spell before being played or w...

- parent_rule: 158
- section_id: 158
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These determine inherent properties and behaviors of the spell before being played or while on the chain.

### [official-rule] 158.2 - These keywords are:

- parent_rule: 158
- section_id: 158
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These keywords are:

### [official-rule] 158.2.a - Action

- parent_rule: 158.2
- section_id: 158
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Action

### [official-rule] 158.2.a.1 - This indicates that, in addition to being able to be played during an Open State, this...

- parent_rule: 158.2.a
- section_id: 158
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This indicates that, in addition to being able to be played during an Open State, this spell may also be played during Open States during Showdowns. See rule 806. Action for more information.

### [official-rule] 158.2.b - Reaction

- parent_rule: 158.2
- section_id: 158
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Reaction

### [official-rule] 158.2.b.1 - Grants all cases and rules of Action.

- parent_rule: 158.2.b
- section_id: 158
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Grants all cases and rules of Action.

### [official-rule] 158.2.b.2 - In addition to all prior cases, may also be played during all forms of Closed State.

- parent_rule: 158.2.b
- section_id: 158
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In addition to all prior cases, may also be played during all forms of Closed State.

### [official-rule] 158.2.b.3 - This will result in this spell resolving before spells and abilities that are already o...

- parent_rule: 158.2.b
- section_id: 158
- page: 16
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This will result in this spell resolving before spells and abilities that are already on the chain. See rule 813. Reaction for more information.

> Basic Runes

### [official-rule] 163.1 - Basic Runes

- parent_rule: 163
- section_id: 163
- page: 17
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> There are six Basic Runes, each with a Domain corresponding to its name: Fury Rune Calm Rune Mind Rune Body Rune Chaos Rune Order Rune

### [official-rule] 163.2 - Basic Runes

- parent_rule: 163
- section_id: 163
- page: 17
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Basic Rune always has the following two Abilities:

### [official-rule] 163.2.a - Basic Runes

- parent_rule: 163.2
- section_id: 163
- page: 17
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> [E]: [Reaction] — Add [1].

### [official-rule] 163.2.b - Basic Runes

- parent_rule: 163.2
- section_id: 163
- page: 17
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Recycle this: [Reaction] — Add [C].

### [official-rule] 163.2.b.1 - Basic Runes

- parent_rule: 163.2.b
- section_id: 163
- page: 17
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Power added this way corresponds to the Domain of the Rune that is being Recycled.

### [official-rule] 164 - Rune Pools

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 164
- page: 17
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Rune Pools

### [official-rule] 165 - The Rune Pool is a conceptual collection of a player's available Energy and Power avail...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 165
- page: 17
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Rune Pool is a conceptual collection of a player's available Energy and Power available to pay Costs.

### [official-rule] 165.1 - When a card adds Energy or Power, it is added to the controlling player's Rune Pool.

- parent_rule: 165
- section_id: 165
- page: 18
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a card adds Energy or Power, it is added to the controlling player's Rune Pool.

### [official-rule] 165.2 - Players must first add Energy and Power to their Rune Pool in order to be able to spend...

- parent_rule: 165
- section_id: 165
- page: 18
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players must first add Energy and Power to their Rune Pool in order to be able to spend it to play cards or pay for Abilities with costs.

### [official-rule] 165.3 - Energy and Power do not have a physical marker or tracker, although players may wish to...

- parent_rule: 165
- section_id: 165
- page: 18
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Energy and Power do not have a physical marker or tracker, although players may wish to use a physical tracker if they are retaining unspent Energy and Power over the course of their turn.

### [official-rule] 166 - Every player's Rune Pool empties at the end of each player's draw phase and the end of...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 166
- page: 18
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Every player's Rune Pool empties at the end of each player's draw phase and the end of each player's turn.

### [official-rule] 166.1 - Any unspent Energy or Power are lost.

- parent_rule: 166
- section_id: 166
- page: 18
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Any unspent Energy or Power are lost.

### [official-rule] 167 - All Abilities that include the action "Add" are abilities that are adding Energy or Pow...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 167
- page: 18
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> All Abilities that include the action "Add" are abilities that are adding Energy or Power to the Rune Pool. See rule 429. Add for more information.

- parent_rule: 169

- page: 18

> Battlefields can be referenced in different states in card text:

### [official-rule] 169.10.a - Battlefields can be “occupied.” This means they have a Unit present.

- section_id: 169
- page: 18
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

- section_id: 169
- page: 18

- section_id: 169
- page: 18

> Battlefields can be “open.” This means they are unoccupied and uncontrolled.

### [official-rule] 176 - Tokens

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 176
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens

### [official-rule] 177 - Tokens are Game Objects created by spells and abilities during play.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 177
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens are Game Objects created by spells and abilities during play.

### [official-rule] 178 - Tokens can be represented by anything. Printed tokens are included in Riftbound booster...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 178
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens can be represented by anything. Printed tokens are included in Riftbound booster packs, but they are not required to play a token.

### [official-rule] 179 - A token's controller is the controller of the spell or ability that created it, unless...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 179
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A token's controller is the controller of the spell or ability that created it, unless the token's type innately determines control or that spell or ability specifies that a different player is the token's controller.

### [official-rule] 180 - A token's owner is the player who controlled the effect that created it.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 180
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A token's owner is the player who controlled the effect that created it.

### [official-rule] 181 - The effect that creates a token may specify the conditions or circumstances under which...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 181
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The effect that creates a token may specify the conditions or circumstances under which it enters the board. These stipulations may alter the usual steps for playing a card.

### [official-rule] 181.1 - The effect may state that the token enters ready or exhausted, if that state is contrar...

- parent_rule: 181
- section_id: 181
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The effect may state that the token enters ready or exhausted, if that state is contrary to the default for the token's type.

### [official-rule] 181.2 - The effect may restrict the location to which the token may be played.

- parent_rule: 181
- section_id: 181
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The effect may restrict the location to which the token may be played.

### [official-rule] 181.3 - The effect may grant temporary abilities or modifications to the token.

- parent_rule: 181
- section_id: 181
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The effect may grant temporary abilities or modifications to the token.

### [official-rule] 182 - Tokens are not cards.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 182
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens are not cards.

### [official-rule] 182.1 - Tokens have some properties in common with cards.

- parent_rule: 182
- section_id: 182
- page: 19
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens have some properties in common with cards.

### [official-rule] 182.1.a - Tokens are played by their owner if their card type is played, following all the applic...

- parent_rule: 182.1
- section_id: 182
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens are played by their owner if their card type is played, following all the applicable steps for playing a card plus any restrictions or modifications from the effect that created the token. See rule 349. Playing Cards for more information.

### [official-rule] 182.1.b - Token units have a Might.

- parent_rule: 182.1
- section_id: 182
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Token units have a Might.

### [official-rule] 182.1.c - Tokens may have one or more tags.

- parent_rule: 182.1
- section_id: 182
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens may have one or more tags.

### [official-rule] 182.1.d - Tokens have a type. They follow all rules for their type unless otherwise specified. Ex...

- parent_rule: 182.1
- section_id: 182
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens have a type. They follow all rules for their type unless otherwise specified. Example: A token unit is a unit. It enters exhausted, can take the standard move action, deals damage equal to its Might in combat, is destroyed if it takes damage equal to or greater than its might, can be chosen or otherwise affected by spells or abilities that choose or affect units, etc.

### [official-rule] 182.1.e - Tokens inherit the recycle destination of their type. Example: Token runes will be recy...

- parent_rule: 182.1
- section_id: 182
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens inherit the recycle destination of their type. Example: Token runes will be recycled to the Rune Deck. Token units, gear, and spells will be recycled to the Main Deck. Token battlefields and legends can’t be recycled.

### [official-rule] 182.2 - Tokens differ from cards in some ways.

- parent_rule: 182
- section_id: 182
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens differ from cards in some ways.

### [official-rule] 182.2.a - Tokens do not have costs.

- parent_rule: 182.2
- section_id: 182
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens do not have costs.

### [official-rule] 182.2.a.1 - Although tokens do not have costs, their cost is treated as being 0 for all purposes.

- parent_rule: 182.2.a
- section_id: 182
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Although tokens do not have costs, their cost is treated as being 0 for all purposes.

### [official-rule] 182.2.b - Tokens do not have domains.

- parent_rule: 182.2
- section_id: 182
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens do not have domains.

### [official-rule] 183 - Tokens are Created on the board or the Chain and cannot exist elsewhere.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 183
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens are Created on the board or the Chain and cannot exist elsewhere.

### [official-rule] 183.1 - If a token is put into any Non-Board Zone besides the chain, it ceases to exist immedia...

- parent_rule: 183
- section_id: 183
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a token is put into any Non-Board Zone besides the chain, it ceases to exist immediately after moving to its new zone.

### [official-rule] 184 - The spell or ability that Creates a token specifies some of its characteristics. It may...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 184
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The spell or ability that Creates a token specifies some of its characteristics. It may have other characteristics, as listed below.

### [official-rule] 184.1 - A 1 [M] Recruit token is a domainless unit token with 1 Might and the Recruit tag.

- parent_rule: 184
- section_id: 184
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A 1 [M] Recruit token is a domainless unit token with 1 Might and the Recruit tag.

### [official-rule] 184.2 - A 3 [M] Sprite token with Temporary is a domainless unit token with 3 Might, the Fae ta...

- parent_rule: 184
- section_id: 184
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A 3 [M] Sprite token with Temporary is a domainless unit token with 3 Might, the Fae tag, and the Temporary keyword. See rule 816. Temporary for more information.

### [official-rule] 184.3 - A 2 [M] Sand Soldier token is a domainless unit token with 2 Might and the Shurima tag.

- parent_rule: 184
- section_id: 184
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A 2 [M] Sand Soldier token is a domainless unit token with 2 Might and the Shurima tag.

### [official-rule] 184.4 - A 3 [M] Mech token is a domainless unit token with 3 Might and the Mech tag.

- parent_rule: 184
- section_id: 184
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A 3 [M] Mech token is a domainless unit token with 3 Might and the Mech tag.

### [official-rule] 184.5 - A Gold gear token is a domainless gear token with “[Reaction][>] Kill this, [E]: [Add]...

- parent_rule: 184
- section_id: 184
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Gold gear token is a domainless gear token with “[Reaction][>] Kill this, [E]: [Add] [A].”

### [official-rule] 184.6 - A 0 [M] Reflection token is a domainless unit token with 0 Might.

- parent_rule: 184
- section_id: 184
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A 0 [M] Reflection token is a domainless unit token with 0 Might.

### [official-rule] 184.7 - A 1 [M] Bird token is a domainless unit token with 1 Might, the Bird tag, and the Defle...

- parent_rule: 184
- section_id: 184
- page: 20
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:
