Examples: "When you conquer here, you may spend a buff to draw 1." "At the end of your turn, ready 2 runes." “The first time I move each turn, you may ready something else that's exhausted.”

### [official-rule] 383.1.a - The phrases that identify triggered abilities do not always appear at the beginning of...

- parent_rule: 383.1
- section_id: 383
- page: 45
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The phrases that identify triggered abilities do not always appear at the beginning of sentences or abilities.

### [official-rule] 383.1.b - If an ability triggers “the [Nth] time” something happens and that trigger condition is...

- parent_rule: 383.1
- section_id: 383
- page: 45
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If an ability triggers “the [Nth] time” something happens and that trigger condition is met multiple times simultaneously, the ability’s controller picks one of those instances to serve as the trigger condition. The ability triggers only once, due to the chosen condition. Example: Wraith of Echoes reads “The first time another friendly unit dies each turn, draw 1.” That ability hasn’t triggered yet this turn. Two other friendly units die simultaneously (say, due to combat damage). The Wraith’s controller chooses one of those deaths to trigger Wraith’s ability.

### [official-rule] 383.2 - Triggered Abilities have a Condition and an Effect.

- parent_rule: 383
- section_id: 383
- page: 45
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Triggered Abilities have a Condition and an Effect.

### [official-rule] 383.2.a - The Condition follows the When.

- parent_rule: 383.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 45
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Condition follows the When.

### [official-rule] 383.2.b - The Effect is the Instruction that follows the comma after the Condition.

- parent_rule: 383.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 45
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Effect is the Instruction that follows the comma after the Condition.

### [official-rule] 383.2.c - The Condition of a Trigger is evaluated after a potentially inciting event has been pro...

- parent_rule: 383.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 45
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Condition of a Trigger is evaluated after a potentially inciting event has been processed.

### [official-rule] 383.2.c.1 - If a Game Object with a Triggered Ability that is active in a specific zone, it is eval...

- parent_rule: 383.2.c
- section_id: 383
- page: 45
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Game Object with a Triggered Ability that is active in a specific zone, it is evaluated and subsequently triggered if it enters that zone at the same time that its Trigger’s condition is met. Example: Immortal Phoenix says “When you kill a unit with a spell, you may pay [1][C] to play me from your trash.” This ability triggers if Immortal Phoenix is in your trash immediately after you kill a unit with a spell, even if the unit you killed with a spell was that Immortal Phoenix.

### [official-rule] 383.2.c.2 - A Game Object will not be able to successfully be able to evaluate its Trigger Conditio...

- parent_rule: 383.2.c
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Game Object will not be able to successfully be able to evaluate its Trigger Condition, however, if it leaves the zone that its Trigger is active from at the same time that its Trigger is satisfied. Example: Viktor, Leader says “When another non-Recruit unit you control dies, play a 1 [M] Recruit unit token into your base.” This ability triggers if Viktor is on the board immediately after another non-Recruit unit you control dies. It does not trigger if Viktor and another non-Recruit unit you control die during the same game action (for instance, if they are both killed in the same Cleanup due to the damage dealt by Unchecked Power).

### [official-rule] 383.3 - When a Condition is met, a Triggered Ability behaves like an Activated Ability and is p...

- parent_rule: 383
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a Condition is met, a Triggered Ability behaves like an Activated Ability and is placed on the Chain.

### [official-rule] 383.3.a - If a Triggered Ability says “you may” as the first part of its Effect, the controller o...

- parent_rule: 383.3
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Triggered Ability says “you may” as the first part of its Effect, the controller of its source will choose whether or not to place the Triggered Ability on the chain when its trigger Condition is fulfilled.

### [official-rule] 383.3.b - If a Triggered Ability contains a cost within instructions, that cost is treated as the...

- parent_rule: 383.3
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Triggered Ability contains a cost within instructions, that cost is treated as the base cost of the Triggered Ability.

### [official-rule] 383.3.b.1 - The cost must be paid in order to finalize the Triggered Ability to the Chain.

- parent_rule: 383.3.b
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The cost must be paid in order to finalize the Triggered Ability to the Chain.

### [official-rule] 383.3.b.2 - Costs within instructions for Triggered Abilities are not paid on resolution, unlike co...

- parent_rule: 383.3.b
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Costs within instructions for Triggered Abilities are not paid on resolution, unlike costs within instructions for Spells.

### [official-rule] 383.3.c - Triggered Abilities can be put on the Chain during Closed States or Open States on any...

- parent_rule: 383.3
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Triggered Abilities can be put on the Chain during Closed States or Open States on any player's turn.

### [official-rule] 383.3.d - If more than one Triggered Ability is Triggered simultaneously, then the player that co...

- parent_rule: 383.3
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If more than one Triggered Ability is Triggered simultaneously, then the player that controls the Abilities selects the order to place them on the Chain.

### [official-rule] 383.3.d.1 - If multiple players separately control Triggered Abilities that are Triggered simultane...

- parent_rule: 383.3.d
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If multiple players separately control Triggered Abilities that are Triggered simultaneously, then starting with the Turn Player and proceeding in Turn Order, each player orders their Triggered Abilities on the Chain.

### [official-rule] 383.3.e - If a triggered ability has a conditional statement following the Condition, that condit...

- parent_rule: 383.3
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a triggered ability has a conditional statement following the Condition, that conditional statement must be true in order for the trigger to be placed on the Chain. Example: Sona, Harmonious reads “At the end of your turn, if I'm at a battlefield, ready up to 4 friendly runes.” Her Trigger Ability’s Condition will be fulfilled in the Ending Step, but the Triggered Ability will only be placed on the chain if she is located at a battlefield when the Condition is fulfilled.

### [official-rule] 383.3.f - Some Triggered Abilities will trigger “once each turn.”

- parent_rule: 383.3
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Some Triggered Abilities will trigger “once each turn.”

### [official-rule] 383.3.f.1 - When the Condition is fulfilled, the player who controls the source of the Triggered Ab...

- parent_rule: 383.3.f
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When the Condition is fulfilled, the player who controls the source of the Triggered Ability may choose to place it on the chain.

### [official-rule] 383.3.f.2 - If they do not, it has not Triggered this turn.

- parent_rule: 383.3.f
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If they do not, it has not Triggered this turn.

### [official-rule] 383.4 - Some Conditions are commonly used and structured in a way that explicitly defines their...

- parent_rule: 383
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Some Conditions are commonly used and structured in a way that explicitly defines their use and other properties of the Effect that is associated with it.

### [official-rule] 383.4.a - Play Effects are Triggered Abilities with the Condition that the Permanent that has the...

- parent_rule: 383.4
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Play Effects are Triggered Abilities with the Condition that the Permanent that has the Play Effect being played to the board.

### [official-rule] 383.4.a.1 - These are commonly structured as “When you play me…” for Units and “When you play this…...

- parent_rule: 383.4.a
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These are commonly structured as “When you play me…” for Units and “When you play this…” for Gear.

### [official-rule] 383.4.a.2 - These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Permanent the...

- parent_rule: 383.4.a
- section_id: 383
- page: 46
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Permanent these effects correspond to is finalized and enters the board.

### [official-rule] 383.4.a.3 - These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Play Effects.

- parent_rule: 383.4.a
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Play Effects.

### [official-rule] 383.4.a.4 - Abilities that trigger when another object is played are not considered Play Effects.

- parent_rule: 383.4.a
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Abilities that trigger when another object is played are not considered Play Effects.

### [official-rule] 383.4.b - Targeting Effects are Triggered Abilities with the Condition that a Game Object becomes...

- parent_rule: 383.4
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Targeting Effects are Triggered Abilities with the Condition that a Game Object becomes targeted.

### [official-rule] 383.4.b.1 - These are commonly structured as “When you choose me …” or “When you choose a [Game Obj...

- parent_rule: 383.4.b
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These are commonly structured as “When you choose me …” or “When you choose a [Game Object] …”

### [official-rule] 383.4.b.2 - These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after a spell or abilit...

- parent_rule: 383.4.b
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after a spell or ability that targets an appropriate Game Object is Finalized.

### [official-rule] 383.4.b.3 - Although these abilities say “choose” in their Condition, they trigger specifically whe...

- parent_rule: 383.4.b
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Although these abilities say “choose” in their Condition, they trigger specifically when an appropriate Game Object is Targeted. See rule 355.6. Targeting for more information on what counts as Targeting.

### [official-rule] 383.4.b.4 - These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Targeting Effects.

- parent_rule: 383.4.b
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Targeting Effects.

### [official-rule] 383.4.c - Conquer Effects are Triggered Abilities with a Condition of a Unit participating in, an...

- parent_rule: 383.4
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Conquer Effects are Triggered Abilities with a Condition of a Unit participating in, and successfully Conquering a Battlefield.

### [official-rule] 383.4.c.1 - These are commonly structured as “When I conquer…” and “When you conquer…”

- parent_rule: 383.4.c
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These are commonly structured as “When I conquer…” and “When you conquer…”

### [official-rule] 383.4.c.2 - This category of Triggered Abilities encompasses only those that are triggered from Uni...

- parent_rule: 383.4.c
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This category of Triggered Abilities encompasses only those that are triggered from Units that were present during the Conquer action, or Abilities that reference the player that performed the Conquer action.

### [official-rule] 383.4.c.2.a - The Conquer Abilities of Units are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit(s)...

- parent_rule: 383.4.c.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Conquer Abilities of Units are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit(s) these effects correspond to are present at a Battlefield when a player gains control of it and gains 1 Victory Point from Conquering.

### [official-rule] 383.4.c.2.b - The Conquer Abilities of anything that references the player Conquering is put on the C...

- parent_rule: 383.4.c.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Conquer Abilities of anything that references the player Conquering is put on the Chain as a Pending Item when the Condition that the player that controls the triggering source has performed a Conquer and gained 1 Victory Point.

### [official-rule] 383.4.c.2.c - If the act of gaining one point from Conquering is negated or replaced in any way, the...

- parent_rule: 383.4.c.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the act of gaining one point from Conquering is negated or replaced in any way, the Conquer Effect will still trigger.

### [official-rule] 383.4.c.3 - These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Conquer Effects.

- parent_rule: 383.4.c
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Conquer Effects.

### [official-rule] 383.4.d - Hold Effects are Triggered Abilities with a Condition of a Unit being present at a Batt...

- parent_rule: 383.4
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Hold Effects are Triggered Abilities with a Condition of a Unit being present at a Battlefield during the Beginning phase when a player scores Victory Points from Holding.

### [official-rule] 383.4.d.1 - These are commonly structured as “When I hold…” or “When you hold…”

- parent_rule: 383.4.d
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These are commonly structured as “When I hold…” or “When you hold…”

### [official-rule] 383.4.d.2 - This category of Triggered Abilities encompasses only those that are triggered from Uni...

- parent_rule: 383.4.d
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This category of Triggered Abilities encompasses only those that are triggered from Units that were present during the Hold action, or Abilities that reference the player that performed the Hold action.

### [official-rule] 383.4.d.2.a - The Hold Abilities of Units are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit these...

- parent_rule: 383.4.d.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Hold Abilities of Units are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit these effects correspond to are present at a Battlefield when a player maintains control of it and Gains 1 Victory Point during their Beginning Phase from Holding.

### [official-rule] 383.4.d.2.b - The Hold Abilities of anything that references the player Holding is put on the Chain a...

- parent_rule: 383.4.d.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 47
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Hold Abilities of anything that references the player Holding is put on the Chain as a Pending Item when the Condition that the player that controls the triggering source has performed a Hold and gained 1 Victory Point.

### [official-rule] 383.4.d.2.c - If the act of gaining one point from Holding is negated or replaced in any way, the Hol...

- parent_rule: 383.4.d.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the act of gaining one point from Holding is negated or replaced in any way, the Hold Effect will still trigger.

### [official-rule] 383.4.d.3 - These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Hold Effects.

- parent_rule: 383.4.d
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Hold Effects.

### [official-rule] 383.4.e - Attack Triggers are Triggered Abilities that trigger when a Unit gains the Attacker des...

- parent_rule: 383.4
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Attack Triggers are Triggered Abilities that trigger when a Unit gains the Attacker designation for the first time during a combat.

### [official-rule] 383.4.e.1 - These are commonly structured as “When I attack…”

- parent_rule: 383.4.e
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These are commonly structured as “When I attack…”

### [official-rule] 383.4.e.2 - These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit these ef...

- parent_rule: 383.4.e
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit these effects correspond to gains the Attacker designation during Combat.

### [official-rule] 383.4.e.2.a - These triggers will only have their condition checked once per combat, despite a Unit b...

- parent_rule: 383.4.e.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These triggers will only have their condition checked once per combat, despite a Unit being able to gain and lose the Attacker designation multiple times in the same combat.

### [official-rule] 383.4.e.2.b - If the trigger condition contains other requirements besides attacking and if those req...

- parent_rule: 383.4.e.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the trigger condition contains other requirements besides attacking and if those requirements are not fulfilled when the unit gains the Attacker designation, it will not trigger in that combat.

### [official-rule] 383.4.e.3 - These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Attack Triggers.

- parent_rule: 383.4.e
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Attack Triggers.

### [official-rule] 383.4.f - Defend Triggers are Triggered Abilities that trigger when a Unit gains the Defender des...

- parent_rule: 383.4
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Defend Triggers are Triggered Abilities that trigger when a Unit gains the Defender designation for the first time during a combat.

### [official-rule] 383.4.f.1 - These are commonly structured as “When I defend…”

- parent_rule: 383.4.f
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These are commonly structured as “When I defend…”

### [official-rule] 383.4.f.2 - These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit these ef...

- parent_rule: 383.4.f
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Triggered Abilities are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit these effects correspond to gains the Defender designation during Combat.

### [official-rule] 383.4.f.2.a - These triggers will only have their condition checked once per combat, despite a Unit b...

- parent_rule: 383.4.f.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These triggers will only have their condition checked once per combat, despite a Unit being able to gain and lose the Defender designation multiple times in the same combat.

### [official-rule] 383.4.f.2.b - If the trigger condition contains other requirements besides defending and if those req...

- parent_rule: 383.4.f.2
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the trigger condition contains other requirements besides defending and if those requirements are not fulfilled when the unit gains the Defender designation, it will not trigger in that combat.

### [official-rule] 383.4.f.3 - These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Defend Triggers.

- parent_rule: 383.4.f
- section_id: 383
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These Triggered Abilities can be referred to as Defend Triggers.

### [official-rule] 384 - Presence on Permanents

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 384
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Presence on Permanents

### [official-rule] 384.1 - Presence on Permanents

- parent_rule: 384
- section_id: 384
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Typically active while on the Board.

### [official-rule] 384.2 - Presence on Permanents

- parent_rule: 384
- section_id: 384
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Triggered Abilities of Permanents are only able to have their Conditions evaluated while on the Board.

### [official-rule] 385 - Presence on Cards outside of the Board

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 385
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Presence on Cards outside of the Board

### [official-rule] 385.1 - Presence on Cards outside of the Board

- parent_rule: 385
- section_id: 385
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Triggered Abilities on cards outside of the Board rely on the Information Level of the zone they are in.

### [official-rule] 385.2 - Presence on Cards outside of the Board

- parent_rule: 385
- section_id: 385
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Triggered Abilities outside of the Board will self-describe their context. Example: The triggered ability "When you conquer, you may discard 1 to return this from your trash to your hand." triggers while the card it's on is in the trash, and not anywhere else.

### [official-rule] 386 - Reflexive Triggers

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 386
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Reflexive Triggers

### [official-rule] 387 - Reflexive Triggers are a type of Triggered Ability that create one or more Chain Items...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 387
- page: 48
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Reflexive Triggers are a type of Triggered Ability that create one or more Chain Items when their condition is met.

### [official-rule] 387.1 - Reflexive Triggers can be recognized by the phrase “Do this:” or “Do one of the followi...

- parent_rule: 387
- section_id: 387
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Reflexive Triggers can be recognized by the phrase “Do this:” or “Do one of the following:”.

### [official-rule] 387.1.a - “Do this” can be followed by “N times.” The Reflexive Trigger will thus be added to the...

- parent_rule: 387.1
- section_id: 387
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> “Do this” can be followed by “N times.” The Reflexive Trigger will thus be added to the chain N times when its condition is met.

### [official-rule] 387.2 - Reflexive Triggers will be preceded by their conditions, if any. If no condition is pre...

- parent_rule: 387
- section_id: 387
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Reflexive Triggers will be preceded by their conditions, if any. If no condition is present in the ability then the Reflexive Trigger will always be added to the Chain.

### [official-rule] 387.3 - If present, the Condition of a Reflexive Trigger will follow the same format as a Trigg...

- parent_rule: 387
- section_id: 387
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If present, the Condition of a Reflexive Trigger will follow the same format as a Triggered Ability.

### [official-rule] 388 - Reflexive Triggers use the Chain.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 388
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Reflexive Triggers use the Chain.

### [official-rule] 388.1 - A new ability is created and added to the chain as a Pending Item. See rule 398. Playin...

- parent_rule: 388
- section_id: 388
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A new ability is created and added to the chain as a Pending Item. See rule 398. Playing or Activating Abilities for more information.

### [official-rule] 388.2 - If a Reflexive Trigger creates more than one Pending Item it creates them all in order,...

- parent_rule: 388
- section_id: 388
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Reflexive Trigger creates more than one Pending Item it creates them all in order, but does not go beyond the first step of adding them to the Chain. See rule 398. Playing or Activating Abilities for more information.

### [official-rule] 389 - Delayed Abilities

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 389
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Delayed Abilities

### [official-rule] 390 - Delayed Abilities are a type of Ability whose trigger Condition identifies a specific t...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 390
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Delayed Abilities are a type of Ability whose trigger Condition identifies a specific time during a turn or a specific event that can occur during a specific timeframe.

### [official-rule] 390.1 - Delayed Abilities can be any other type of Ability, and contain all of the properties o...

- parent_rule: 390
- section_id: 390
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Delayed Abilities can be any other type of Ability, and contain all of the properties of that type in addition to the properties of Delayed Abilities.

### [official-rule] 390.2 - Delayed Triggers are Triggered Abilities that can be recognized by describing a specifi...

- parent_rule: 390
- section_id: 390
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Delayed Triggers are Triggered Abilities that can be recognized by describing a specific time of the turn, or by structuring a Triggered Ability with a specific frame of time as a restriction.

### [official-rule] 390.3 - Delayed Replacements are Replacement Effects that can be recognized by specifying the e...

- parent_rule: 390
- section_id: 390
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Delayed Replacements are Replacement Effects that can be recognized by specifying the effect they are replacing at a specific time, or “the [Nth] time” in the description of the effect as it resolves.

### [official-rule] 390.4 - Delayed Passive Abilities are Passive Abilities that are applicable only during a speci...

- parent_rule: 390
- section_id: 390
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Delayed Passive Abilities are Passive Abilities that are applicable only during a specified window of time. The time that the Delayed Passive Ability applies will be recognized in the effect that initiates it.

### [official-rule] 390.5 - Delayed Linked Abilities are Linked Abilities that are generated by another Ability and...

- parent_rule: 390
- section_id: 390
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Delayed Linked Abilities are Linked Abilities that are generated by another Ability and reference that Ability or Game Objects it affects. Delayed Linked Abilities don’t necessarily identify a specific window of time, and instead are defined by the Ability they are linked with.

### [official-rule] 390.5.a - If that Ability affects a Game Object, the Delayed Linked Ability’s window will be as l...

- parent_rule: 390.5
- section_id: 390
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If that Ability affects a Game Object, the Delayed Linked Ability’s window will be as long as that Game Object is in an appropriate zone.

### [official-rule] 390.5.b - If the Delayed Linked Ability references the source of the abilities, its window will b...

- parent_rule: 390.5
- section_id: 390
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the Delayed Linked Ability references the source of the abilities, its window will be as long as the source is in an appropriate zone. Refl391. Delayed Abilities will resolve or be active just like the ability they augment, but only during the specified time in the effect that created the Delayed Ability. Example: Ravenborn Tome reads “The next spell you play this turn deals 1 Bonus Damage” is a Delayed Passive Ability that passively adds 1 damage to just the next spell played. The next spell is a specific time, and the 1 Bonus Damage is a passive ability. Example: Noxian Guillotine reads “Choose a unit. Kill it the next time it takes damage this turn.” When the chosen unit takes damage is the specified time, and killing it is the condition for a Delayed Triggered Ability.

### [official-rule] 392 - Delayed Abilities are not associated with Units or Gear; they are created by other Abil...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 392
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Delayed Abilities are not associated with Units or Gear; they are created by other Abilities or Spells. As such they are executed when their condition and/or specified time occurs regardless of whether the source of the Delayed Ability is still on the board or not.

### [official-rule] 393 - Linked Abilities

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 393
- page: 49
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Linked Abilities

### [official-rule] 394 - Linked Abilities are a set of Abilities with one or more of the component Abilities ref...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 394
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Linked Abilities are a set of Abilities with one or more of the component Abilities referencing the other Abilities in the set.

### [official-rule] 395 - In order for a set of Abilities to be Linked, they must be present in the printed Effec...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 395
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In order for a set of Abilities to be Linked, they must be present in the printed Effect or Rules Text of the same Game Object, or be granted by the same source to another Game Object.

### [official-rule] 396 - Linked Abilities can contain component Abilities of any type.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 396
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Linked Abilities can contain component Abilities of any type.

### [official-rule] 397 - A component Linked Ability that references a Game Object affected by another Ability in...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 397
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A component Linked Ability that references a Game Object affected by another Ability in the set may only interact with Game Objects affected by the Abilities it is Linked with.

### [official-rule] 398 - Playing or Activating Abilities

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 398
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Playing or Activating Abilities

### [official-rule] 399 - Playing or activating Abilities follows the same steps of playing cards.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 399
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Playing or activating Abilities follows the same steps of playing cards.

### [official-rule] 400 - Abilities when added to the Chain become Pending Items until they complete the steps of...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 400
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Abilities when added to the Chain become Pending Items until they complete the steps of Playing.

### [official-rule] 400.1 - When an Ability finishes the steps of playing it becomes a Chain Item just like a Spell.

- parent_rule: 400
- section_id: 400
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When an Ability finishes the steps of playing it becomes a Chain Item just like a Spell.

### [official-rule] 400.2 - When an Ability with the [Add] action is finalized it resolves immediately instead of b...

- parent_rule: 400
- section_id: 400
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When an Ability with the [Add] action is finalized it resolves immediately instead of becoming a Chain Item, like a Unit or Gear.

### [official-rule] 401 - 1. Activate or trigger the Ability

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 401
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 1. Activate or trigger the Ability

### [official-rule] 401.1 - 1. Activate or trigger the Ability

- parent_rule: 401
- section_id: 401
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Add a Pending Item to the chain representing the Ability that is either being Activated or Triggered. Notably, although this Chain Item will not have a card representing it, this will create a Closed State. See rule 349. Playing Cards for more information.

### [official-rule] 401.2 - 1. Activate or trigger the Ability

- parent_rule: 401
- section_id: 401
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If there is currently a game effect being resolved, continue resolving the game effect instead of continuing the following steps.

### [official-rule] 402 - 2. Make relevant choices

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 402
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 2. Make relevant choices

### [official-rule] 402.1 - 2. Make relevant choices

- parent_rule: 402
- section_id: 402
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Make all choices required for this ability, such as targets, modes, or other relevant decisions. See rule 349. Playing Cards for more information

### [official-rule] 402.2 - 2. Make relevant choices

- parent_rule: 402
- section_id: 402
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If legal options are not available for an Activated Ability, it is not legal to activate it.

### [official-rule] 402.3 - 2. Make relevant choices

- parent_rule: 402
- section_id: 402
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If there are not enough options to make legal choices for a Triggered Ability that has been put on the chain, remove it from the Chain now. It ceases to be a Pending Item but never becomes a Chain Item.

### [official-rule] 402.3.a - 2. Make relevant choices

- parent_rule: 402.3
- section_id: 402
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is not an Ability being countered.

### [official-rule] 402.3.b - 2. Make relevant choices

- parent_rule: 402.3
- section_id: 402
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If there are legal options to choose, the ability’s controller must choose them. They may not decline this stage of playing a Trigger.

### [official-rule] 403 - 3. Determine Total Cost

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 403
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 3. Determine Total Cost

### [official-rule] 403.1 - 3. Determine Total Cost

- parent_rule: 403
- section_id: 403
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Determine the base cost of the Ability.

### [official-rule] 403.1.a - 3. Determine Total Cost

- parent_rule: 403.1
- section_id: 403
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Activated Abilities will have a cost listed before the “:” in their text.

### [official-rule] 403.1.b - 3. Determine Total Cost

- parent_rule: 403.1
- section_id: 403
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Triggered Abilities will typically not have a base cost associated with them when placed on the chain due to their conditions.

### [official-rule] 403.1.b.1 - 3. Determine Total Cost

- parent_rule: 403.1.b
- section_id: 403
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Triggered Ability has a cost within instructions (e.g. “[do X] to [do Y]”), the cost is taken as the base cost. See rule 742.1. for more information on costs within instructions.

### [official-rule] 403.2 - 3. Determine Total Cost

- parent_rule: 403
- section_id: 403
- page: 50
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Apply cost increases and decreases as a result of choices made in the prior step. See rule 349. Playing Cards for more information.

### [official-rule] 403.3 - 3. Determine Total Cost

- parent_rule: 403
- section_id: 403
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Apply any other cost increases or decreases as necessary. See rule 349. Playing Cards for more information.

### [official-rule] 404 - 4. Pay Costs

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 404
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 4. Pay Costs

### [official-rule] 404.1 - 4. Pay Costs

- parent_rule: 404
- section_id: 404
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Pay costs as determined in the prior step. See rule 349. Playing Cards for more information.

### [official-rule] 404.2 - 4. Pay Costs

- parent_rule: 404
- section_id: 404
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> At this stage, players may decline to pay for Triggered Abilities that have incurred a cost. If they do, the ability will cease being a Pending Item and be removed from the Chain. It never becomes a Chain Item.

### [official-rule] 404.2.a - 4. Pay Costs

- parent_rule: 404.2
- section_id: 404
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is not an Ability being countered.

### [official-rule] 405 - 5. Check Legality

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 405
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 5. Check Legality

### [official-rule] 405.1 - 5. Check Legality

- parent_rule: 405
- section_id: 405
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Ensure that the Ability’s targets are still legal.

### [official-rule] 405.2 - 5. Check Legality

- parent_rule: 405
- section_id: 405
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Ensure that the Ability’s effect would not create an illegal state. If it would, resolve in the same way you would resolve a Card that creates an illegal state. See rule 349. Playing Cards for more information.

### [official-rule] 406 - 6. Proceed with Play

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 406
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 6. Proceed with Play

### [official-rule] 406.1 - 6. Proceed with Play

- parent_rule: 406
- section_id: 406
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This Ability is no longer Pending.

### [official-rule] 406.2 - 6. Proceed with Play

- parent_rule: 406
- section_id: 406
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This Ability becomes a Chain Item.

### [official-rule] 406.3 - 6. Proceed with Play

- parent_rule: 406
- section_id: 406
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If there are other Pending Items on the Chain, their controllers perform the remaining steps of playing now.

### [official-rule] 406.4 - 6. Proceed with Play

- parent_rule: 406
- section_id: 406
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Other players have an opportunity to play Reactions before the resolution of spells. See rule 327. Chains for more information.

### [official-rule] 406.5 - 6. Proceed with Play

- parent_rule: 406
- section_id: 406
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Otherwise, execute the Ability just like a Spell, then clear the Chain Item from the Chain.

### [official-rule] 407 - Game Actions

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 407
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Game Actions

### [official-rule] 408 - Game Actions are actions players may perform at any given time during the game.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 408
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Game Actions are actions players may perform at any given time during the game.

### [official-rule] 409 - A player, unless otherwise specified or prompted, may only perform actions on their turn.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 409
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A player, unless otherwise specified or prompted, may only perform actions on their turn.

### [official-rule] 410 - There are two types of Game Actions:

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 410
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> There are two types of Game Actions:

### [official-rule] 410.1 - There are two types of Game Actions:

- parent_rule: 410
- section_id: 410
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Discretionary Actions

### [official-rule] 410.1.a - There are two types of Game Actions:

- parent_rule: 410.1
- section_id: 410
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Discretionary Action is a Game Action that may be performed at any time during a player's turn during a Neutral Open State. See rule 307. States of the Turn for more information.

### [official-rule] 410.1.b - There are two types of Game Actions:

- parent_rule: 410.1
- section_id: 410
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A player may take any number of Discretionary Actions available to them during their turn.

### [official-rule] 410.1.b.1 - There are two types of Game Actions:

- parent_rule: 410.1.b
- section_id: 410
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> As long as any conditions, if applicable, are met.

### [official-rule] 410.1.b.2 - There are two types of Game Actions:

- parent_rule: 410.1.b
- section_id: 410
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> As long as any costs, if applicable, are paid.

### [official-rule] 410.1.b.3 - There are two types of Game Actions:

- parent_rule: 410.1.b
- section_id: 410
- page: 51
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> As long as it does not result in any forbidden action being taken or game state being created. Example: A player can't use the Standard Move discretionary action to move a unit to a Battlefield that's already occupied by Units controlled by two other players.

### [official-rule] 410.2 - There are two types of Game Actions:

- parent_rule: 410
- section_id: 410
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Limited Actions

### [official-rule] 410.2.a - There are two types of Game Actions:

- parent_rule: 410.2
- section_id: 410
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Limited Action is a game action that a spell, ability, or circumstance of the turn's progression causes the player to perform. A player cannot perform these actions at-will.

### [official-rule] 410.2.b - There are two types of Game Actions:

- parent_rule: 410.2
- section_id: 410
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These actions can only be taken when a player is instructed to do so as the execution of an effect or during their specified occasion during the turn. Example: A player may Draw during the Draw Step of the Beginning Phase of their turn or when instructed to do so by a spell or ability. They can't choose to Draw at any other time.

### [official-rule] 411 - Responsibility

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 411
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Responsibility

### [official-rule] 411.1 - Responsibility

- parent_rule: 411
- section_id: 411
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Game Actions may be the responsibility of up to one player. The player that performs the Game Action is responsible for it.

### [official-rule] 411.2 - Responsibility

- parent_rule: 411
- section_id: 411
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In the case where a Game Action is performed by procedures of the game and not by any player, that Game Action is not the responsibility of any player.

### [official-rule] 411.3 - Responsibility

- parent_rule: 411
- section_id: 411
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Certain rules assign responsibility to players for Game Actions that they are not normally responsible for.

### [official-rule] 411.4 - Responsibility

- parent_rule: 411
- section_id: 411
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If an ability triggers when “you” do something, it triggers when a Game Action that you are responsible for occurs. Example: A gear reads “When you move an enemy unit, you may exhaust this to [Stun] it.” It will trigger whenever a move game action that you are responsible for causes an enemy unit to move. If an opponent plays a spell that reads “choose a Battlefield. Each player may move a unit they don’t control to that battlefield,” and a player that controls the gear chooses to move an enemy unit, their gear will trigger.

### [official-rule] 411.5 - Responsibility

- parent_rule: 411
- section_id: 411
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Note that certain Game Actions, specifically Kill, can be attributed to spells and abilities. This attribution is not the same as the responsibility a given player has for the kill game action.

### [official-rule] 411.5.a - Responsibility

- parent_rule: 411.5
- section_id: 411
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spells and abilities have attribution for game actions, while players have responsibility.

### [official-rule] 412 - Types of Actions

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 412
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Types of Actions

### [official-rule] 413 - Draw

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Draw

### [official-rule] 413.1 - Draw

- parent_rule: 413
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Drawing a card takes a single card from a zone and adding it to the player's Hand.

### [official-rule] 413.1.a - Draw

- parent_rule: 413.1
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Unless specified otherwise, drawing takes cards from the Main Deck.

### [official-rule] 413.2 - Draw

- parent_rule: 413
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 413.2.a - Draw

- parent_rule: 413.2
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Each player draws 1 during the Draw Step of their Beginning Phase.

### [official-rule] 413.2.b - Draw

- parent_rule: 413.2
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The player may draw cards when instructed to do so by other game effects.

### [official-rule] 413.3 - Draw

- parent_rule: 413
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action, when instructed, is formatted as "Draw X."

### [official-rule] 413.4 - Draw

- parent_rule: 413
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a player attempts to draw more cards than are available in their Main Deck, they do the following:

### [official-rule] 413.4.a - Draw

- parent_rule: 413.4
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Draw as many as possible.

### [official-rule] 413.4.b - Draw

- parent_rule: 413.4
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Perform a Burn Out. See rule 431. Burn Out for more information.

### [official-rule] 413.4.c - Draw

- parent_rule: 413.4
- section_id: 413
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Draw the remaining cards needed to complete the Draw action.

### [official-rule] 414 - Exhaust

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 414
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Exhaust

### [official-rule] 414.1 - Exhaust

- parent_rule: 414
- section_id: 414
- page: 52
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Exhausting is an action that marks a non-spell Game Object on the board as "spent."

### [official-rule] 414.1.a - Exhaust

- parent_rule: 414.1
- section_id: 414
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> To mark it, rotate the card 90 degrees opposite of Readying, so that the orientation of the card is lengthwise in front of you.

### [official-rule] 414.1.b - Exhaust

- parent_rule: 414.1
- section_id: 414
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Unit that is already Exhausted cannot be Exhausted again.

### [official-rule] 414.1.c - Exhaust

- parent_rule: 414.1
- section_id: 414
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Unit is instructed to be Exhausted while it is already Exhausted, nothing additional happens.

### [official-rule] 414.2 - Exhaust

- parent_rule: 414
- section_id: 414
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> "Exhausted" is a state for Game Objects on the board that other game effects and rules can reference.

### [official-rule] 414.3 - Exhaust

- parent_rule: 414
- section_id: 414
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 414.3.a - Exhaust

- parent_rule: 414.3
- section_id: 414
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Most Exhaust actions will be costs for Activated Abilities or Discretionary Actions. Example: A unit's Standard Move exhausts the unit as a cost.

### [official-rule] 414.4 - Exhaust

- parent_rule: 414
- section_id: 414
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When Exhausting is listed as a Cost, then the Action must be able to be completed for the cost to be paid. Example: A spell says "As an additional cost to play this, you may exhaust a friendly unit." An exhausted friendly unit may not be exhausted again as the additional cost for the spell, and the additional cost has not been paid.

### [official-rule] 414.5 - Exhaust

- parent_rule: 414
- section_id: 414
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In abilities, the Exhaust symbol represents the cost "Exhaust this" or "Exhaust me." It resembles a card turning sideways.

### [official-rule] 415 - Ready

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 415
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Ready

### [official-rule] 415.1 - Ready

- parent_rule: 415
- section_id: 415
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Readying is an action that marks a non-spell Game Object on the board as available for action.

### [official-rule] 415.1.a - Ready

- parent_rule: 415.1
- section_id: 415
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> To mark it, rotate the card 90 degrees opposite of Exhausting, so that it is vertically oriented in front of you.

### [official-rule] 415.1.b - Ready

- parent_rule: 415.1
- section_id: 415
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Unit that is already Ready cannot be Readied again.

### [official-rule] 415.1.c - Ready

- parent_rule: 415.1
- section_id: 415
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Unit is instructed to be Readied while it is already Ready, nothing additional happens.

### [official-rule] 415.2 - Ready

- parent_rule: 415
- section_id: 415
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> "Ready" is a state for Game Objects on the board that other game effects and rules can reference.

### [official-rule] 415.3 - Ready

- parent_rule: 415
- section_id: 415
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 415.3.a - Ready

- parent_rule: 415.3
- section_id: 415
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A player Readies all non-spell Game Objects they Control during the Ready Step of the Beginning Phase on their turn.

### [official-rule] 415.3.b - Ready

- parent_rule: 415.3
- section_id: 415
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may also Ready Game Objects on the board when effects or spells instruct them to do so.

### [official-rule] 416 - Recycle

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 416
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Recycle

### [official-rule] 416.1 - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416
- section_id: 416
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Recycling cards is the action in which a player takes one or more cards from a specific zone and then puts it on the bottom of the corresponding deck.

### [official-rule] 416.1.a - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416.1
- section_id: 416
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Main Deck cards are Recycled to the Main Deck.

### [official-rule] 416.1.b - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416.1
- section_id: 416
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Runes are Recycled to the Rune Deck.

### [official-rule] 416.1.c - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416.1
- section_id: 416
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Each player Recycles cards to their own Main Deck and Rune Deck, regardless of which player is instructed to perform the Recycle action.

### [official-rule] 416.2 - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416
- section_id: 416
- page: 53
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 416.2.a - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416.2
- section_id: 416
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The player must Recycle cards from the specified zones when instructed to do so by game effects or costs.

### [official-rule] 416.3 - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416
- section_id: 416
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When Recycling is listed as a Cost, the action must be able to be completed for the cost to be paid. Example: Vi, Destructive has the ability "Recycle 1 from your trash: Give me +1 [M] this turn." Each time a player activates the ability, they must recycle 1 card from their trash to pay its cost. If they have no cards in their trash, they can't activate the ability, because they can't pay its cost.

### [official-rule] 416.4 - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416
- section_id: 416
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When Recycling is part of an effect, a player must Recycle as many cards as possible from the specified zone or zones.

### [official-rule] 416.5 - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416
- section_id: 416
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If 2 or more cards are Recycled to the Main Deck simultaneously, they are placed on the bottom of that deck in a random order. Example: Garbage Grabber has the ability "Recycle 3 from your trash, [1], [E]: Draw " To pay the cost of activating this ability, its controller chooses 3 cards from their trash and places them on the bottom of their Main Deck in a random order.

### [official-rule] 416.5.a - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416.5
- section_id: 416
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If 2 or more cards are Recycled to the Rune Deck simultaneously, they are placed on the bottom of that deck in the order of their owner's choosing.

### [official-rule] 416.6 - Recycle

- parent_rule: 416
- section_id: 416
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action, when instructed, is sometimes formatted as "Recycle X from [Zone]." That means to take X cards of the instructed player's choice from the relevant zone and recycle them. Example: Dr. Mundo, Expert has the ability "At the start of your Beginning Phase, recycle 3 from your trash." As that ability resolves, its controller recycles 3 cards from their trash. If there are fewer than 3 cards in that player's trash, they recycle as many as they can.

### [official-rule] 417 - Deal

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Deal

### [official-rule] 417.1 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spells, Units, Abilities, and other game effects may Deal Damage to units.

### [official-rule] 417.1.a - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.1
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Assigning Damage during the Combat Damage Step is not Dealing Damage, but will cause Damage to be Dealt when assignment is complete.

### [official-rule] 417.1.b - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.1
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> To Deal Damage to Units, mark the specified amount of Damage on the Unit.

### [official-rule] 417.1.c - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.1
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage is marked on each unit separately.

### [official-rule] 417.1.d - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.1
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage can be Dealt to more than one Unit at the same time.

### [official-rule] 417.1.e - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.1
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Valid Damage is a positive integer amount, greater than or equal to 1 Damage.

### [official-rule] 417.1.e.1 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.1.e
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Only Valid Damage is Dealt. Example: A unit has “when I take damage, give me +2 [M] this turn.” A spell is played that Prevents the next 3 damage the unit would take. If a player plays Hextech Ray targeting the unit, it will take no damage and its triggered ability will not trigger. If that player had played Void Seeker instead, it would be Dealt 1 and trigger its ability. Example: A unit is present at Void Gate. A spell is played that Prevents the next 3 damage the unit would take. If a player plays Hextech Ray targeting the unit, it will take no damage - in order to apply the Bonus Damage from Void Gate, the Deal game action would have to occur, and Hextech Ray did not Deal any damage.

### [official-rule] 417.2 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Only Damage can be Dealt.

### [official-rule] 417.3 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Dealing Damage is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 417.3.a - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.3
- section_id: 417
- page: 54
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Assigning Damage causes Damage to be dealt outside of being directed to Deal Damage. See rule 454. Combat for more information.

### [official-rule] 417.4 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Dealing can have the intrinsic property of Bonus Damage.

### [official-rule] 417.5 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Bonus Damage is a property granted to the action of Dealing and alters the amount of Damage distributed by this action. See rule 712. Bonus Damage for more information.

### [official-rule] 417.6 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Deal actions can originate from one or more sources.

### [official-rule] 417.6.a - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.6
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a game effect does not specify a source, the game effect describing the Deal action is the source. Example: Void Seeker is a spell that reads “Deal 4 to a unit at a battlefield. Draw 1.” The damage that Void Seeker instructs you to deal is dealt by Void Seeker.

### [official-rule] 417.6.b - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.6
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a game effect does specify a source, then that source is what is considered the origin of the Damage for this Deal action.

### [official-rule] 417.6.b.1 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.6.b
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units and Spells can be the source of Damage for Deal actions.

### [official-rule] 417.6.b.2 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.6.b
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Abilities can be the source of Damage for Deal actions.

### [official-rule] 417.6.b.2.a - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.6.b.2
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When an Ability is the source of Damage for a Deal action, it is in addition to the Spell or Unit that created that Ability. Example: Iron Ballista is a gear that says “[E]: Deal 2 to a unit at a battlefield.” This damage is dealt both by a gear and by an ability.

### [official-rule] 417.6.b.3 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.6.b
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a spell or ability specifies a Unit as the source of the Damage for the Deal action, it is not in addition to the spell or ability that specified it. Example: Challenge is a spell that reads “Choose a friendly unit and an enemy unit. They deal damage equal to their Mights to each other.” The damage that Challenge causes to be dealt is dealt by the chosen units, not by Challenge.

### [official-rule] 417.6.c - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.6
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage Dealt as a result of being assigned during Combat has the Units as its source.

### [official-rule] 417.6.c.1 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417.6.c
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Damage assigned, and subsequently Dealt, to attackers has the defenders as the source and vice versa.

### [official-rule] 417.7 - Deal

- parent_rule: 417
- section_id: 417
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Deal actions can distribute Damage as part of combat actions or non-combat actions.

### [official-rule] 418 - Heal

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 418
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Heal

### [official-rule] 418.1 - Heal

- parent_rule: 418
- section_id: 418
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage being cleared from Units is Healing.

### [official-rule] 418.1.a - Heal

- parent_rule: 418.1
- section_id: 418
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If Damage is cleared for any reason it is considered Healing.

### [official-rule] 418.2 - Heal

- parent_rule: 418
- section_id: 418
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> More than one Unit can be Healed at the same time.

### [official-rule] 418.3 - Heal

- parent_rule: 418
- section_id: 418
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Healing is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 418.3.a - Heal

- parent_rule: 418.3
- section_id: 418
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Heal units when game effects instruct them to, or at specific points in the turn.

### [official-rule] 419 - Play

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 419
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Play

### [official-rule] 419.1 - Play

- parent_rule: 419
- section_id: 419
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A player Plays cards by placing them on the chain and queuing them to be finalized. See rule 349. Playing Cards for more information on playing cards. See rule 337. Finalize for more information on finalizing cards.

### [official-rule] 419.1.a - Play

- parent_rule: 419.1
- section_id: 419
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> By default, a player can only Play cards from their hand or their Chosen Champion zone.

### [official-rule] 419.2 - Play

- parent_rule: 419
- section_id: 419
- page: 55
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is a Discretionary Action.

### [official-rule] 419.2.a - Play

- parent_rule: 419.2
- section_id: 419
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> As long as a player has the resources to pay the costs associated with the card and legal choices to make for their cards, they may Play cards.

### [official-rule] 419.3 - Play

- parent_rule: 419
- section_id: 419
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Game effects may result in cards being played as part of their resolution.

### [official-rule] 419.3.a - Play

- parent_rule: 419.3
- section_id: 419
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This treats Play as a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 419.3.b - Play

- parent_rule: 419.3
- section_id: 419
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Treat all steps of Play as normal, except as noted by the game effect creating this Limited Play Effect.

### [official-rule] 419.3.c - Play

- parent_rule: 419.3
- section_id: 419
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If there are no eligible cards to Play when instructed to Play in this manner like this, then nothing happens and resolution continues.

### [official-rule] 419.4 - Play

- parent_rule: 419
- section_id: 419
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Some Abilities trigger when cards are played or otherwise check whether cards have been played.

### [official-rule] 419.4.a - Play

- parent_rule: 419.4
- section_id: 419
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> These abilities trigger when the act of playing the card has been completed by the resolution of the card.

### [official-rule] 419.4.b - Play

- parent_rule: 419.4
- section_id: 419
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a game effect prevents the resolution of the card—for example, because the card was countered—the card wasn't played and no abilities that trigger on playing cards trigger. See rule 425. Counter for more information.

### [official-rule] 420 - Move

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 420
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Move

### [official-rule] 420.1 - Move

- parent_rule: 420
- section_id: 420
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Moving is the act of a Game Object moving between two Locations on The Board. See rule 440. Movement for more information on movement.

### [official-rule] 420.2 - Move

- parent_rule: 420
- section_id: 420
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Moving is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 420.2.a - Move

- parent_rule: 420.2
- section_id: 420
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only move Game Objects when instructed to do so by Game Effects or costs.

### [official-rule] 420.2.b - Move

- parent_rule: 420.2
- section_id: 420
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Standard Move inherent to Units may also cause Movement. See rule 144. for more information on the Standard Move.

### [official-rule] 420.3 - Move

- parent_rule: 420
- section_id: 420
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Standard Move inherent to Units is a Discretionary Action.

### [official-rule] 420.3.a - Move

- parent_rule: 420.3
- section_id: 420
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Cost is Exhausting one or more Units.

### [official-rule] 420.3.b - Move

- parent_rule: 420.3
- section_id: 420
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Effect is Moving those Unit.

### [official-rule] 421 - Hide

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 421
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Hide

### [official-rule] 421.1 - Hide

- parent_rule: 421
- section_id: 421
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Hiding a card is the act of placing a card facedown at a Battlefield you control.

### [official-rule] 421.2 - Hide

- parent_rule: 421
- section_id: 421
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Hiding is a Discretionary Action.

### [official-rule] 421.2.a - Hide

- parent_rule: 421.2
- section_id: 421
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Hidden keyword allows a player to pay a cost to Hide a card any time they have a Hidden card in their hand or Champion Zone and meet the prerequisites. See rule 811. Hidden for more information.

### [official-rule] 421.3 - Hide

- parent_rule: 421
- section_id: 421
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Cards that are facedown at Battlefields have their gameplay properties and permissions defined by the effect that put them there. Example: Cards that players Hide with the Hidden keyword can be Played for [0] on any subsequent turn as a property of the Hidden keyword.

### [official-rule] 421.4 - Hide

- parent_rule: 421
- section_id: 421
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a facedown card would change zones or if the game ends, its owner reveals it to all players. See rule 128. Privacy for more information.

### [official-rule] 422 - Discard

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 422
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Discard

### [official-rule] 422.1 - Discard

- parent_rule: 422
- section_id: 422
- page: 56
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Discarding a card is moving it from a player's hand directly into their trash without activating or executing its normal rules text.

### [official-rule] 422.1.a - Discard

- parent_rule: 422.1
- section_id: 422
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The player who is performing the action chooses which cards to send to their Trash, and may use Private Information to do so.

### [official-rule] 422.1.b - Discard

- parent_rule: 422.1
- section_id: 422
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> “When I am discarded” abilities or other Triggered Abilities that trigger on discarding are executed after discarding has occurred.

### [official-rule] 422.2 - Discard

- parent_rule: 422
- section_id: 422
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Discarding is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 422.2.a - Discard

- parent_rule: 422.2
- section_id: 422
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A player must Discard cards when instructed to do so by game effects or costs.

### [official-rule] 422.3 - Discard

- parent_rule: 422
- section_id: 422
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When Discarding is listed as a Cost, then the Action must be able to be completed for the cost to be paid. Example: A card has the ability "Discard 2: Deal 2 damage to a unit at a battlefield." To activate the ability, the card's controller must have at least 2 cards in hand and must be able to discard them.

### [official-rule] 422.4 - Discard

- parent_rule: 422
- section_id: 422
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When Discarding is part of an effect, then a player must Discard as many cards as possible from their hand. If instructed to discard more cards than they have in their hand, further discard instructions are ignored. Example: Undercover Agent has the ability "[Deathknell][>] Discard 2, then draw 2. (When I die, get the effect.)" If Undercover Agent's controller has 2 or more cards in hand, they must discard 2. If they have 1 card in hand, they discard 1, and the rest of the discard instruction is ignored. If they have no cards in hand, the entire discard instruction is ignored. Regardless of how many cards they discard, they then draw 2.

### [official-rule] 422.5 - Discard

- parent_rule: 422
- section_id: 422
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action is formatted as "Discard X."

### [official-rule] 423 - Stun

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 423
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Stun

### [official-rule] 423.1 - Stun

- parent_rule: 423
- section_id: 423
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Stunning is the act of selecting one or more Units on the Board and rendering them Stunned.

### [official-rule] 423.1.a - Stun

- parent_rule: 423.1
- section_id: 423
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Stunned is a binary state. A Unit is Stunned or it isn't.

### [official-rule] 423.1.a.1 - Stun

- parent_rule: 423.1.a
- section_id: 423
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Stunned Unit can not be Stunned again. Example: Eclipse Herald has the ability "When you stun an enemy unit, ready me and give me +1 [M] this turn." Its controller plays a spell that reads "Stun a unit." They may choose a unit that's already stunned, but if they do, Eclipse Herald will not trigger.

### [official-rule] 423.1.a.2 - Stun

- parent_rule: 423.1.a
- section_id: 423
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Stunned Units lose the Stunned status at the beginning of the next Ending Step.

### [official-rule] 423.1.b - Stun

- parent_rule: 423.1
- section_id: 423
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Stunned Unit does not contribute its might to damage in the combat damage step. See rule 460. The Combat Damage Step for more information.

### [official-rule] 423.1.c - Stun

- parent_rule: 423.1
- section_id: 423
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Stunned Unit must still have damage applied to it equal to, or greater than, its full might value to be killed.

### [official-rule] 423.2 - Stun

- parent_rule: 423
- section_id: 423
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Stunning is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 423.2.a - Stun

- parent_rule: 423.2
- section_id: 423
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Stun Units when directed to by Game Effects.

### [official-rule] 424 - Reveal

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 424
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Reveal

### [official-rule] 424.1 - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424
- section_id: 424
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Revealing is the act of presenting a card to all players from a zone that one or more players do not have access to the information of.

### [official-rule] 424.1.a - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424.1
- section_id: 424
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Revealed is a temporary state and is not a zone.

### [official-rule] 424.1.a.1 - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424.1.a
- section_id: 424
- page: 57
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Other cards, including the card being revealed, can reference the act of being Revealed.

### [official-rule] 424.1.a.2 - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424.1.a
- section_id: 424
- page: 57-58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Cards remain in the zone they are being Revealed from. Example: If a card is being Revealed from the top of a player's Main Deck, it is still the top card of that player's Main Deck.

### [official-rule] 424.1.b - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424.1
- section_id: 424
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Unless otherwise described, Revealed cards do nothing else beyond become temporarily known information to all players.

### [official-rule] 424.2 - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424
- section_id: 424
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Revealing is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 424.2.a - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424.2
- section_id: 424
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Reveal cards from Private or Secret zones when instructed to do so by Game Effects.

### [official-rule] 424.2.b - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424.2
- section_id: 424
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> During the course of a game of Riftbound, a player may choose to show Private information to one or more other players. This does not count as revealing and does not trigger any effects that trigger when cards are revealed.

### [official-rule] 424.3 - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424
- section_id: 424
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action is formatted as "Reveal cards from [zone]." Example: "Reveal 2 cards from the top of your Main Deck" would be executed by taking the two cards from the top of your Main Deck, and then presenting them to all players to clearly read and understand at the table. Then, when all players have had a chance to understand the revealed information, return them to the top of the deck in the same order.

### [official-rule] 424.4 - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424
- section_id: 424
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:
