> Game Effects can manipulate or modify the cards Revealed while they are Revealed

### [official-rule] 424.4.a - Reveal

- parent_rule: 424.4
- section_id: 424
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> While cards are Revealed, those cards can be further manipulated, or accessed. They can even have their destination modified for when the Reveal ends. Example: "Reveal 2 cards from the top of your Main Deck. Put one into your hand and Recycle the other" would be executed by taking the top two cards from your Main Deck to the table, and once every player present has had a chance to understand them you would make the selection of one of them to add to your hand. The one you did not select would then be Recycled to the bottom of the Main Deck.

### [official-rule] 425 - Counter

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 425
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Counter

### [official-rule] 425.1 - Counter

- parent_rule: 425
- section_id: 425
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Countering is the act of negating the execution, activation, or otherwise playing of a card or ability by a player.

### [official-rule] 425.1.a - Counter

- parent_rule: 425.1
- section_id: 425
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A card or ability that is Countered does nothing and is cleared from the chain.

### [official-rule] 425.1.b - Counter

- parent_rule: 425.1
- section_id: 425
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A card that is Countered is not considered to have been played.

### [official-rule] 425.1.c - Counter

- parent_rule: 425.1
- section_id: 425
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Countering does not refund any costs paid to play a card, activate an ability, or trigger an ability.

### [official-rule] 425.1.c.1 - Counter

- parent_rule: 425.1.c
- section_id: 425
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This includes additional costs.

### [official-rule] 425.2 - Counter

- parent_rule: 425
- section_id: 425
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Countering is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 425.2.a - Counter

- parent_rule: 425.2
- section_id: 425
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Counter cards and abilities when directed to by Game Effects.

### [official-rule] 425.3 - Counter

- parent_rule: 425
- section_id: 425
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action is formatted as "Counter [a card or ability on the chain]."

### [official-rule] 426 - Buff

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 426
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Buff

### [official-rule] 426.1 - Buff

- parent_rule: 426
- section_id: 426
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Buffing is the action of placing a Buff counter on a Unit. See rule 701. Buffs for more information.

### [official-rule] 426.1.a - Buff

- parent_rule: 426.1
- section_id: 426
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Buff is also an object.

### [official-rule] 426.1.b - Buff

- parent_rule: 426.1
- section_id: 426
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> To Buff a unit, place a Buff Counter on it if it does not have one already

### [official-rule] 426.1.b.1 - Buff

- parent_rule: 426.1.b
- section_id: 426
- page: 58
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the unit already has a Buff Counter on it, it does not get another one.

### [official-rule] 426.1.c - Buff

- parent_rule: 426.1
- section_id: 426
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units with Buff Counters can still be chosen for actions that Buff units, but will not be Buffed as part of the execution. Example: A spell reads "Buff a unit. Then, if it was buffed this way, draw a card." It will place a buff counter on a unit that has no buff counter, and then its controller will draw a card. However, if the player chooses a unit with a buff counter on it already, then the card will not be drawn.

### [official-rule] 426.2 - Buff

- parent_rule: 426
- section_id: 426
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Buffing is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 426.2.a - Buff

- parent_rule: 426.2
- section_id: 426
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Buff units when Game Effects direct them to do so.

### [official-rule] 426.3 - Buff

- parent_rule: 426
- section_id: 426
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action is formatted as "Buff [one or more units]." e.g., "Buff a unit." e.g., "Buff a friendly unit." e.g., "Buff two friendly units at the same battlefield."

### [official-rule] 427 - Banish

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 427
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Banish

### [official-rule] 427.1 - Banish

- parent_rule: 427
- section_id: 427
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Banishing is the action of placing a card from any zone to Banishment. See 107.5. Banishment for more information.

### [official-rule] 427.2 - Banish

- parent_rule: 427
- section_id: 427
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a card is Banished it is placed directly into the Banishment zone from its origin.

### [official-rule] 427.2.a - Banish

- parent_rule: 427.2
- section_id: 427
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Banish is not a subset of Kill.

### [official-rule] 427.2.b - Banish

- parent_rule: 427.2
- section_id: 427
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Banish is not a subset of Discard.

### [official-rule] 427.3 - Banish

- parent_rule: 427
- section_id: 427
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Cards and effects can refer to cards that were banished by the same object.

### [official-rule] 427.3.a - Banish

- parent_rule: 427.3
- section_id: 427
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Separate instances of an object or effect banishing cards do not reference other banished cards by objects of the same name or effects originating from cards of the same name. Example: A spell reads "Banish the top card of your Main Deck. When you conquer this turn, draw it." This card effect allows you to draw the banished card from banishment if a condition is met. If more than one of these effects is played, each one triggers separately. Each card will be drawn separately. If the turn player passes the turn without playing the card, the card remains in banishment indefinitely. If the same player plays another copy of the same spell, banishes another card, and satisfies its condition, they could play the new banished card, but not the original banished card. See rule 393. Linked Abilities for more information.

### [official-rule] 427.4 - Banish

- parent_rule: 427
- section_id: 427
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Banishing is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 427.4.a - Banish

- parent_rule: 427.4
- section_id: 427
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Banish cards or permanents when Game Effects direct them to do so.

### [official-rule] 427.5 - Banish

- parent_rule: 427
- section_id: 427
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action is formatted as "Banish [one or more permanents or cards]." e.g., "Banish a card from your hand." e.g., "Banish 2 cards from your trash." e.g., "Look at the top 2 cards of your Main Deck. Draw one of them and banish the other."

### [official-rule] 428 - Kill

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 428
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Kill

### [official-rule] 428.1 - Kill

- parent_rule: 428
- section_id: 428
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Killing is the action of a Permanent going to the trash from the board.

### [official-rule] 428.1.a - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.1
- section_id: 428
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This can be Active or Passive.

### [official-rule] 428.1.a.1 - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.1.a
- section_id: 428
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Active Kill is when the action is taken when instructed by a game effect or as a cost for a card or ability.

### [official-rule] 428.1.a.1.a - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.1.a.1
- section_id: 428
- page: 59
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is referred to as a Kill Instruction.

### [official-rule] 428.1.a.1.b - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.1.a.1
- section_id: 428
- page: 59-60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a unit with a Deathknell is to be put in the Trash due to a Kill Instruction, it first has its Deathknell added to the chain as a Pending Item. Note the unit’s location, attributes, and other relevant information to process its Deathknell when finalized before completing this Kill Instruction.

### [official-rule] 428.1.a.2 - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.1.a
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Passive Kill is when the action is taken as a result of Lethal Damage or as a consequence for any other state.

### [official-rule] 428.2 - Kill

- parent_rule: 428
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a permanent is killed it is placed directly in the trash from its place of origin.

### [official-rule] 428.2.a - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.2
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> It is only considered Killed if its origin was any zone on the board.

### [official-rule] 428.2.b - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.2
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is not a subset of Move.

### [official-rule] 428.3 - Kill

- parent_rule: 428
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Killing is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 428.3.a - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.3
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Kill units when Game Effects direct them to do so.

### [official-rule] 428.4 - Kill

- parent_rule: 428
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Killing can also be the result of resolving a Cleanup.

### [official-rule] 428.5 - Kill

- parent_rule: 428
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Killing can be attributed to one or more Game Objects.

### [official-rule] 428.5.a - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.5
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Killed Unit or Gear is said to be Killed by that Game Object.

### [official-rule] 428.5.b - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.5
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A spell or ability that contains a Kill instruction is responsible for Killing the Unit or Gear.

### [official-rule] 428.5.c - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.5
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When one or more Units is killed due to a Cleanup, that kill action is attributed to the spell or ability that resolved immediately prior to that Cleanup that dealt damage to the Unit or Units. The player responsible for the deal action is responsible for the kill action.

### [official-rule] 428.5.d - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.5
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Abilities originating from Game Objects that are attributed Kill Actions are attributed in addition to the Game Object that created them. Example: There is a spell that says “Do this twice: Deal 3 to a unit.” Immortal Phoenix is a unit that says “When you kill a unit with a spell, you may pay [1][C] to play me from your trash.” A player plays the spell while Immortal Phoenix is in their trash. The “do this” phrasing means that it has a reflexive triggered ability, which places two triggered abilities on the chain. As each of those triggered abilities resolve, it deals damage to the unit chosen for that ability. If one of these abilities deals lethal damage to a unit, both the spell and its ability are considered sources of the damage, and so both the spell and its ability receive attribution for killing the unit. This means that the spell’s controller killed a unit with a spell, so Immortal Phoenix’s ability will trigger.

### [official-rule] 428.5.e - Kill

- parent_rule: 428.5
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In order to Kill something “with” a spell or ability, the Kill action must be attributed to the spell or ability, the player must control that spell or ability, and the player must be responsible for the Kill action.

### [official-rule] 428.6 - Kill

- parent_rule: 428
- section_id: 428
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action is formatted as "Kill [one or more permanents]." e.g., "Kill an enemy unit." e.g., "Kill this, [2]: Draw 1." e.g., "Kill all gear."

### [official-rule] 429 - Add

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 429
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Add

### [official-rule] 429.1 - Add

- parent_rule: 429
- section_id: 429
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Adding is the action of putting resources into a player's Rune Pool.

### [official-rule] 429.2 - Add

- parent_rule: 429
- section_id: 429
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spells, triggered abilities, and activated abilities that Add resources finalize immediately.

### [official-rule] 429.2.a - Add

- parent_rule: 429.2
- section_id: 429
- page: 60
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Priority and Focus will not pass from Add abilities being finalized or resolving, and will resolve before any other outstanding items on the chain are finalized.

### [official-rule] 429.3 - Add

- parent_rule: 429
- section_id: 429
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Activated abilities that Add resources and have the Reaction tag can be activated during the playing or resolution of other spells and abilities, any time that those spells or abilities require resources be paid. Example: A player can add Energy and Power through any means before initiating the process of playing a spell. After initiating that process, in the Pay Costs step, they may activate Add Reactions to add Energy or Power to pay costs.

### [official-rule] 429.3.a - Add

- parent_rule: 429.3
- section_id: 429
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When an Add ability is activated in this way, it immediately finalizes and resolves, even during the resolution of spells and abilities.

### [official-rule] 429.4 - Add

- parent_rule: 429
- section_id: 429
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Adding is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 429.4.a - Add

- parent_rule: 429.4
- section_id: 429
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Add resources when Game Effects direct them to do so.

### [official-rule] 429.5 - Add

- parent_rule: 429
- section_id: 429
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action is formatted as "Add [one or more resources]." e.g., "Add [2]." means "Add 2 Energy." e.g., "[E]: Add [Y]." means "Add 1 Power of the Order domain." e.g., "Add [1][G]." means "Add 1 Energy and 1 Power of the Calm domain."

### [official-rule] 430 - Channel

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 430
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Channel

### [official-rule] 430.1 - Channel

- parent_rule: 430
- section_id: 430
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Channeling is the action of taking one or more Runes from the top of a player's Rune Deck and putting them on the board.

### [official-rule] 430.2 - Channel

- parent_rule: 430
- section_id: 430
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Game Effect that instructs a player to channel 1 or more runes may specify the conditions or circumstances under which those runes enter the board. Example: A spell reads "Channel 1 rune exhausted." As that spell resolves, its controller puts the top rune of their rune deck onto the board and that rune enters the board exhausted rather than ready.

### [official-rule] 430.3 - Channel

- parent_rule: 430
- section_id: 430
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Channeling is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 430.3.a - Channel

- parent_rule: 430.3
- section_id: 430
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only channel runes when Game Effects direct them to do so.

### [official-rule] 430.4 - Channel

- parent_rule: 430
- section_id: 430
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action is formatted as "Channel X rune(s)," optionally followed by conditions or stipulations. e.g., "Channel 1 rune." e.g., "When you play me, channel 1 rune exhausted." e.g., "Channel 2 runes exhausted. If you couldn't channel 2 runes this way, draw 1."

### [official-rule] 431 - Burn Out

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 431
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Burn Out

### [official-rule] 431.1 - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431
- section_id: 431
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Burning Out is an action a player must perform if they attempt to move one or more cards from their Main Deck to any other zone in excess of the number of cards remaining in their Main Deck:

### [official-rule] 431.1.a - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.1
- section_id: 431
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a player must Draw cards in excess to the number of cards in their deck, they will Draw as many as possible, perform this action, then Draw the remaining amount instructed.

### [official-rule] 431.1.b - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.1
- section_id: 431
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a player must put one or more cards from their Main Deck in any other zone, such as the Trash, in excess of the number of cards in their deck they will do so as much as possible, perform this action, and then complete the remaining number required by the instruction.

### [official-rule] 431.1.c - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.1
- section_id: 431
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If an instruction directs a player to look at or reveal cards in excess to the number of cards in a player’s Main Deck, that player looks at or Reveals as many as possible, but does not Burn Out, then proceeds with the rest of the instruction.

### [official-rule] 431.1.c.1 - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.1.c
- section_id: 431
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If there are insufficient cards among the looked at or revealed cards to perform subsequent actions to the revealed or looked at cards, any further instructions are ignored. This does not cause a Burn Out, even if those instructions would cause those cards to change zones. Reminder: Cards are considered in the zone of origin while being looked at or revealed, in this case the Main Deck.

### [official-rule] 431.2 - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431
- section_id: 431
- page: 61
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> To Burn Out, a player does the following in sequence:

### [official-rule] 431.2.a - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.2
- section_id: 431
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Performs as much of the prescribed action as possible.

### [official-rule] 431.2.b - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.2
- section_id: 431
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Recycles their trash into their Main Deck. Reminder: When multiple cards are Recycled to the Main Deck at the same time, those cards must be randomized

### [official-rule] 431.2.c - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.2
- section_id: 431
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Chooses an opponent to gain 1 point.

### [official-rule] 431.2.d - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.2
- section_id: 431
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Completes the remainder of the action that caused them to burn out. Example: A player attempts to draw 1 during their Draw Phase while their Main Deck is empty. That player instead recycles their trash into their Main Deck, randomizing it as normal, then chooses an opponent to gain 1 point, and then draws 1.

### [official-rule] 431.3 - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431
- section_id: 431
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A player's Main Deck may remain empty as they Burn Out, usually because their trash is also empty. When they attempt to perform the original action again, it will cause another Burn Out.

### [official-rule] 431.3.a - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.3
- section_id: 431
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Unless some effect intervenes, this will result in them burning out repeatedly, giving 1 point to an opponent each time, until an opponent passes the Victory Score and wins the game.

### [official-rule] 431.3.b - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.3
- section_id: 431
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Points gained after the first Burn Out being processed in sequence cannot be replaced or prevented by any means. Points gained after the first Burn Out being processed in sequence that cause a player to reach or surpass the Victory Score for their game mode will cause that player to win the game if they also have more points than any opponent. The player wins immediately, without needing to wait for a cleanup to occur.

### [official-rule] 431.4 - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431
- section_id: 431
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Burning Out is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 431.4.a - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431.4
- section_id: 431
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only burn out when Game Effects direct them to do so.

### [official-rule] 431.5 - Burn Out

- parent_rule: 431
- section_id: 431
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Burning Out is a Replacement Effect. See rule 367. Replacement Effects for more information.

### [official-rule] 432 - Double

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 432
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Double

### [official-rule] 432.1 - Double

- parent_rule: 432
- section_id: 432
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Doubling is the act of increasing a numeric attribute by an amount equal to that attribute’s current value.

### [official-rule] 432.1.a - Double

- parent_rule: 432.1
- section_id: 432
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This creates an effect that modulates that attribute by that specific amount for the duration specified by the Game Effect that instructed the player to perform this action. Example: A unit with 3 base Might and Shield 2 is in combat as a Defender. Since Shield applies, its current Might is 5. A player chooses it as the target for Last Stand, a spell that reads in part “Double a friendly unit's Might this turn.” Its current Might is 5, so it gets +5 Might this turn, for a current Might of 10. After combat, Shield no longer applies, but the +5 Might from Last Stand does, so the unit’s Might is 8.

### [official-rule] 432.2 - Double

- parent_rule: 432
- section_id: 432
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Doubling is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 432.2.a - Double

- parent_rule: 432.2
- section_id: 432
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Double when Game Effects direct them to do so.

### [official-rule] 433 - Swap

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 433
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Swap

### [official-rule] 433.1 - Swap

- parent_rule: 433
- section_id: 433
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Swapping is the act of increasing one numeric value and decreasing another numeric value on some number of Game Objects such that their values are reversed.

### [official-rule] 433.1.a - Swap

- parent_rule: 433.1
- section_id: 433
- page: 62
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Swapping creates two different effects that apply to each attribute. One that Increases one value and one that Decreases the other.

### [official-rule] 433.1.b - Swap

- parent_rule: 433.1
- section_id: 433
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> To accomplish this, determine the difference between these values and then apply an Increase for that amount to the lower value of the two attributes, and a Decrease of that amount to the higher value of the two attributes.

### [official-rule] 433.1.c - Swap

- parent_rule: 433.1
- section_id: 433
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If both attributes are the same numeric value, Swapping has no effect.

### [official-rule] 433.2 - Swap

- parent_rule: 433
- section_id: 433
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Swapping is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 433.2.a - Swap

- parent_rule: 433.2
- section_id: 433
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only swap when Game Effects direct them to do so.

### [official-rule] 434 - Attach

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Attach

### [official-rule] 434.1 - Attach

- parent_rule: 434
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Attaching is the act of linking two cards on the board together to combine their effects in some way. This causes one or more cards to become Attached and at least one card to become a Top-Most Card. See rule 716. Attachment for more information.

### [official-rule] 434.1.a - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.1
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Attaching affects two cards at once.

### [official-rule] 434.1.a.1 - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.1.a
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> One card will be designated as the one being Attached, and thus become Attached. The other will become the Top-Most Card.

### [official-rule] 434.1.b - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.1
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is represented by physically laying the Top-Most card on top of the other or others such that all Effect Texts and Might Bonuses are showing, but nothing else from the card or cards Attached.

### [official-rule] 434.1.b.1 - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.1.b
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In the situation where there is more than one card attached to the Top-Most card, they should be stacked in such a way that all Effect Text boxes and Might Bonuses are readable. The order of the Attached cards has no bearing on the application of effects.

### [official-rule] 434.1.c - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.1
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Top-Most card has all Effect Text of all cards Attached to it appended to its Rules Text.

### [official-rule] 434.1.d - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.1
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Top-Most Card has its Might modulated by the Might Bonus of all cards Attached to it.

### [official-rule] 434.1.e - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.1
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Attaching one or more cards will cause those cards’ Rules Text to become Inactive for as long as they remain Attached. See rule 716. Attachment for more information.

### [official-rule] 434.1.f - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.1
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Attaching a card to a new Top-Most Card will cause it to Detach from the card to which it is currently Attached.

### [official-rule] 434.1.g - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.1
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Attaching a card to its current Top-Most Card will not have any effect.

### [official-rule] 434.1.h - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.1
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Game Effect instructs a player to Attach a card to its current Top-Most Card, nothing additional happens.

### [official-rule] 434.2 - Attach

- parent_rule: 434
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Attaching is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 434.2.a - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.2
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Attach cards when directed to by Game Effects.

### [official-rule] 434.3 - Attach

- parent_rule: 434
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Attaching cards does not inherently choose or specify a target. However, Game Effects that Attach cards may do so.

### [official-rule] 434.4 - Attach

- parent_rule: 434
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a card Attaches to a card, its location becomes the same as the new Top-Most Card.

### [official-rule] 434.4.a - Attach

- parent_rule: 434.4
- section_id: 434
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This is not a Move.

### [official-rule] 435 - Detach

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 435
- page: 63
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Detach

### [official-rule] 435.1 - Detach

- parent_rule: 435
- section_id: 435
- page: 63-64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Detaching is the act of unlinking two cards that are currently linked through the act of Attaching. This causes one to cease being Attached, and potentially causes the other to cease being a Top-Most card.

### [official-rule] 435.1.a - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.1
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Detaching affects only cards that are currently Attached to another card.

### [official-rule] 435.1.a.1 - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.1.a
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Game effects that instruct a player to Detach a card that is currently not Attached to anything will do nothing.

### [official-rule] 435.1.b - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.1
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When one or more cards become Detached, they cease to be in the Attached state.

### [official-rule] 435.1.b.1 - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.1.b
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> To represent this, these cards should no longer be placed under the card they were previously placed under as Top-Most Card.

### [official-rule] 435.1.b.2 - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.1.b
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the Top-Most Card no longer has any cards Attached to it after this, it is no longer a Top-Most Card.

### [official-rule] 435.1.c - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.1
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The card being Detached has its Effect Text become Inactive and its Rules Text cease being Inactive.

### [official-rule] 435.1.d - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.1
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Top-Most Card ceases to have the Effect Text of the card being Detached appended to its Rules Text.

### [official-rule] 435.1.e - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.1
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Top-Most Card ceases to have its Might modulated by the Might Bonus of the card being Detached.

### [official-rule] 435.2 - Detach

- parent_rule: 435
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Detaching is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 435.2.a - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.2
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Detach cards when directed to by Game Effects.

### [official-rule] 435.3 - Detach

- parent_rule: 435
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Detaching cards does not inherently choose, or specify a target. However, Game Effects that Detach cards may.

### [official-rule] 435.4 - Detach

- parent_rule: 435
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a card Detaches from a Top-Most Card, its location is the same as the Top-Most Card from which it Detached.

### [official-rule] 435.4.a - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.4
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the Detached card was a Gear and this causes it to become present at a Battlefield, it will be Recalled during the next Cleanup.

### [official-rule] 435.4.b - Detach

- parent_rule: 435.4
- section_id: 435
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the Attached card was Detached because the Top-Most Card changed zones from a board zone to a non-board zone, then the location that the Attached Card will Detach to is the last location the Top-Most Card was at before changing from a board zone to a non-board zone.

### [official-rule] 436 - Predict

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 436
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Predict

### [official-rule] 436.1 - Predict

- parent_rule: 436
- section_id: 436
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Predicting a card is the act of looking at a single card from the top of the Main Deck and choosing whether or not to Recycle it.

### [official-rule] 436.1.a - Predict

- parent_rule: 436.1
- section_id: 436
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When more than one card is Predicted, the Predicting player looks at that many cards and Recycles any number of them.

### [official-rule] 436.1.b - Predict

- parent_rule: 436.1
- section_id: 436
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Player who performs the game action may rearrange any cards not Recycled in this way.

### [official-rule] 436.2 - Predict

- parent_rule: 436
- section_id: 436
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Predicting is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 436.2.a - Predict

- parent_rule: 436.2
- section_id: 436
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Predict cards when instructed to do so by game effects.

### [official-rule] 436.3 - Predict

- parent_rule: 436
- section_id: 436
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action, when instructed, is formatted as "Predict X."

### [official-rule] 436.3.a - Predict

- parent_rule: 436.3
- section_id: 436
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If omitted, X is presumed to be 1.

### [official-rule] 436.4 - Predict

- parent_rule: 436
- section_id: 436
- page: 64
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a player attempts to Predict more cards than are available in their Main Deck, they will Predict as many as possible instead.

### [official-rule] 436.4.a - Predict

- parent_rule: 436.4
- section_id: 436
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Player will not perform a Burn Out as a result of Predicting with too few cards in their deck.

### [official-rule] 437 - Prevent

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Prevent

### [official-rule] 437.1 - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Preventing damage is the act of reducing the Damage a set of game objects would take.

### [official-rule] 437.1.a - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.1
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Prevent is an action that interacts with Damage.

### [official-rule] 437.1.b - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.1
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Prevent appears in statements that define an amount of damage and the source of the damage it will affect, as well as the timespan it will be relevant for.

### [official-rule] 437.1.b.1 - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.1.b
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Prevent actions are usually formatted as “Prevent the next X [source] damage that would be dealt to a [unit] this turn.”

### [official-rule] 437.1.b.1.a - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.1.b.1
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The X is referred to as the Prevent Value.

### [official-rule] 437.1.b.1.b - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.1.b.1
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> X can be “All,” which specifies an infinite amount of damage.

### [official-rule] 437.1.b.2 - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.1.b
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Prevent will always apply to the next damage that would be dealt to a unit affected by the Prevent action.

### [official-rule] 437.2 - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When damage is Prevented, it is replaced with an event where it deals that much damage reduced by the Prevent Value tracked on the Unit specifically.

### [official-rule] 437.2.a - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.2
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The damage being dealt as a result of Preventing can never be less than 0, but can be 0. This is equivalent to not dealing damage.

### [official-rule] 437.3 - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When damage is dealt this way, reduce the Prevent Value being tracked on the Unit affected by the Prevent action by the prevented amount.

### [official-rule] 437.3.a - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.3
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the Prevent Value is 0 or less, then Prevent is no longer being tracked on the Unit in question, and the effect expires.

### [official-rule] 437.3.b - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.3
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The reduced value is the newly tracked Prevent Value.

### [official-rule] 437.3.c - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.3
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the Prevent Value is “All” then it remains “All.”

### [official-rule] 437.4 - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage dealt to a Unit that has that all of that damage Prevented is not considered to have been dealt to it at all.

### [official-rule] 437.5 - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage can still be assigned to Units in combat that are affected by Prevent. The damage dealt as a result of that assignment will be affected by the Prevent action.

### [official-rule] 437.5.a - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.5
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Damage can be assigned to a Unit up to a value that would be Lethal considering the Prevent Value of all Prevent Actions on a Unit. Example: A unit with 2 [M] is being assigned damage in the combat damage step. The unit has “prevent the first 3 damage I would take each combat.” The unit would need to be assigned 5 damage in order to have lethal damage assigned to it.

### [official-rule] 437.5.b - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.5
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> No amount of damage is ever considered lethal if the Prevent Value is “All.”

### [official-rule] 437.6 - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Prevent is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 437.6.a - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437.6
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Prevent Damage when directed to by Game Effects.

### [official-rule] 437.7 - Prevent

- parent_rule: 437
- section_id: 437
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Prevent is a Delayed Replacement Effect. See rule 367. Replacement Effects for more information. See rule 389. Delayed Abilities for more information.

### [official-rule] 438 - Replace

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 438
- page: 65
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Replace

### [official-rule] 438.1 - Replace

- parent_rule: 438
- section_id: 438
- page: 65-66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Replacing is the act of Creating a token in the place of another card or token without playing it while inheriting all effects or statuses of the game object it replaced.

### [official-rule] 438.2 - Replace

- parent_rule: 438
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Replacing is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 438.2.a - Replace

- parent_rule: 438.2
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The player may Replace cards and tokens when instructed to do so by other game effects.

### [official-rule] 438.3 - Replace

- parent_rule: 438
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action, when instructed, is formatted as "Replace [X] with [Y]."

### [official-rule] 438.3.a - Replace

- parent_rule: 438.3
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The [X] is the target to be Replaced.

### [official-rule] 438.3.b - Replace

- parent_rule: 438.3
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The [Y] is the object that will Replace the target.

### [official-rule] 438.3.b.1 - Replace

- parent_rule: 438.3.b
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This will always specify a Token to create.

### [official-rule] 438.4 - Replace

- parent_rule: 438
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Replacing is not a subset of Banishing.

### [official-rule] 438.5 - Replace

- parent_rule: 438
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The card or token that is Replaced is placed in Banishment.

### [official-rule] 438.5.a - Replace

- parent_rule: 438.5
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> While it resides in Banishment, it is considered to have been Replaced and not Banished.

### [official-rule] 438.6 - Replace

- parent_rule: 438
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a token is Replaced it will stop existing once it begins its occupancy in Banishment.

### [official-rule] 438.6.a - Replace

- parent_rule: 438.6
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This does not invalidate the token created, or the act of Replacement.

### [official-rule] 438.7 - Replace

- parent_rule: 438
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Tokens that have been Created through a Replace action can be instructed to be “Swapped back.”

### [official-rule] 438.7.a - Replace

- parent_rule: 438.7
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> “Swapping Back” is an extension of the Replace action.

### [official-rule] 438.7.b - Replace

- parent_rule: 438.7
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> To Swap Back, the token stops existing and the original card is returned to the space that the token just occupied, inheriting all current effects and statuses.

### [official-rule] 438.7.c - Replace

- parent_rule: 438.7
- section_id: 438
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If there is nothing in Banishment to swap back to, usually because a token was replaced, then this object can never swap back.

### [official-rule] 439 - Create

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Create

### [official-rule] 439.1 - Create

- parent_rule: 439
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Creating is the act of producing a Game Object that previously did not exist in the game.

### [official-rule] 439.2 - Create

- parent_rule: 439
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Effects that Create one or more Game Objects will direct where those Game Objects must go - the Game Objects are Created directly to the zones in question.

### [official-rule] 439.2.a - Create

- parent_rule: 439.2
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Prior to being Created, these Game Objects did not exist outside of the zone they were Created to. After being Created, they may change zones as appropriate for Game Objects of their type.

### [official-rule] 439.2.b - Create

- parent_rule: 439.2
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a zone is not specified by the effect, the Game Object will be created to the appropriate zone for its type. Permanents will be Created to any location on the Board that they can be played to.

### [official-rule] 439.2.b.1 - Create

- parent_rule: 439.2.b
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spells will be Created to the Chain.

### [official-rule] 439.2.b.2 - Create

- parent_rule: 439.2.b
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Runes will be Created to base.

### [official-rule] 439.2.b.3 - Create

- parent_rule: 439.2.b
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Legends will be Created to the Legend Zone.

### [official-rule] 439.2.b.4 - Create

- parent_rule: 439.2.b
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Battlefields will be Created to the Battlefield Zone.

### [official-rule] 439.3 - Create

- parent_rule: 439
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> An effect that Creates a Game Object will specify the nature of the Game Object created.

### [official-rule] 439.3.a - Create

- parent_rule: 439.3
- section_id: 439
- page: 66
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If the Game Object is a token, it will follow the normal rules for tokens. See rule 176. Tokens for more information.

### [official-rule] 439.4 - Create

- parent_rule: 439
- section_id: 439
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Unless specified otherwise by the Game Effect that Creates a Game Object, any such Game Object is owned by the player who Created it. Control is established as usual for Game Objects of the appropriate type.

### [official-rule] 439.4.a - Create

- parent_rule: 439.4
- section_id: 439
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Created permanent, rune, legend, or spell is controlled by its owner as it is created.

### [official-rule] 439.4.b - Create

- parent_rule: 439.4
- section_id: 439
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Created battlefield is uncontrolled as it is created.

### [official-rule] 439.5 - Create

- parent_rule: 439
- section_id: 439
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This action, when instructed, is usually formatted as "Create [X] to [Y]" or “Add [X] to [Y].”

### [official-rule] 439.6 - Create

- parent_rule: 439
- section_id: 439
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Creating is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 439.6.a - Create

- parent_rule: 439.6
- section_id: 439
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may only Create Game Objects when directed to by Game Effects.

### [official-rule] 440 - Movement

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 440
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Movement

### [official-rule] 441 - Moving is a Limited Action.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 441
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Moving is a Limited Action.

### [official-rule] 441.1 - A Permanent changing its position from any space on the Board to another space on the B...

- parent_rule: 441
- section_id: 441
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Permanent changing its position from any space on the Board to another space on the Board is a Move, unless it is caused by a corrective Recall. See rule 420. Move for more information.

### [official-rule] 441.2 - A card changing game zones does not in itself constitute a Move.

- parent_rule: 441
- section_id: 441
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A card changing game zones does not in itself constitute a Move.

### [official-rule] 441.3 - Moving is instantaneous.

- parent_rule: 441
- section_id: 441
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Moving is instantaneous.

### [official-rule] 441.3.a - There is no state for Permanents between locations.

- parent_rule: 441.3
- section_id: 441
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> There is no state for Permanents between locations.

### [official-rule] 441.3.b - Permanents are either at their Origin before Moving or their Destination after Moving.

- parent_rule: 441.3
- section_id: 441
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Permanents are either at their Origin before Moving or their Destination after Moving.

### [official-rule] 441.3.c - Moving does not use the Chain, nor is it able to be Reacted to.

- parent_rule: 441.3
- section_id: 441
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Moving does not use the Chain, nor is it able to be Reacted to.

### [official-rule] 442 - Moving is defined by the Origin and Destination of the Permanent that is changing locat...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 442
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Moving is defined by the Origin and Destination of the Permanent that is changing locations.

### [official-rule] 442.1 - The Origin is where the Permanent is starting from.

- parent_rule: 442
- section_id: 442
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Origin is where the Permanent is starting from.

### [official-rule] 442.2 - The Destination is where the Permanent is going to.

- parent_rule: 442
- section_id: 442
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Destination is where the Permanent is going to.

### [official-rule] 442.2.a - In Modes of Play with more than two players, Battlefields with Staged Combats or Combat...

- parent_rule: 442.2
- section_id: 442
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In Modes of Play with more than two players, Battlefields with Staged Combats or Combats in Progress are Invalid Destinations for Moves of all kinds (Standard Moves or otherwise) by Units controlled by Players not involved in those Combats or who don't already have permanents at that Battlefield.

### [official-rule] 442.2.b - In Modes of Play with teammates, Battlefields Controlled by a player’s teammates are In...

- parent_rule: 442.2
- section_id: 442
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In Modes of Play with teammates, Battlefields Controlled by a player’s teammates are Invalid Destinations for Moves of all kinds (Standard Moves or otherwise) by Units that player controls.

### [official-rule] 442.2.c - If an action would require a Move that would cause a Unit to become present in a Locati...

- parent_rule: 442.2
- section_id: 442
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If an action would require a Move that would cause a Unit to become present in a Location where it cannot move for any reason, such as a Battlefield with two players that are not the controller of the Unit performing this Move action are in a Combat or such a Combat is Staged, it instead Recalls. See rule 449. Recalls for more information.

### [official-rule] 442.3 - Units are the only Permanents that can Move.

- parent_rule: 442
- section_id: 442
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units are the only Permanents that can Move.

### [official-rule] 443 - Players may choose to move their Units with the Standard Move. See rule 144. for more i...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 443
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players may choose to move their Units with the Standard Move. See rule 144. for more information on the Standard Move.

### [official-rule] 444 - Spells, Abilities, or other effects may cause a Move to occur.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 444
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Spells, Abilities, or other effects may cause a Move to occur.

### [official-rule] 444.1 - The source of the Move will provide details on any restrictions on legality for Destina...

- parent_rule: 444
- section_id: 444
- page: 67
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The source of the Move will provide details on any restrictions on legality for Destination.

### [official-rule] 444.2 - Units cannot Move to a Battlefield that already has units from 2 other players present...

- parent_rule: 444
- section_id: 444
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units cannot Move to a Battlefield that already has units from 2 other players present by any means.

### [official-rule] 445 - The Destination becomes Contested if it is a Battlefield not controlled by the controll...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 445
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Destination becomes Contested if it is a Battlefield not controlled by the controller of the Unit or Units that moved.

### [official-rule] 446 - Units may cause a Showdown without a Combat when they Move.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 446
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units may cause a Showdown without a Combat when they Move.

### [official-rule] 446.1 - A Showdown is opened when a Move causes a Battlefield to become Contested while it has...

- parent_rule: 446
- section_id: 446
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Showdown is opened when a Move causes a Battlefield to become Contested while it has no Units present from any player other than those that just moved.

### [official-rule] 447 - Units may cause Combat when they Move.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 447
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units may cause Combat when they Move.

### [official-rule] 447.1 - A Combat is opened when a Move causes a Battlefield to become Contested and Units contr...

- parent_rule: 447
- section_id: 447
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Combat is opened when a Move causes a Battlefield to become Contested and Units controlled by opposing players, or when a Move causes Units controlled by opposing players to become present in the same Battlefield where a Showdown is ongoing. See rule 454. Combat for more information.

### [official-rule] 448 - When a Move action is complete, perform a Cleanup.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 448
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When a Move action is complete, perform a Cleanup.

### [official-rule] 449 - Recalls

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 449
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Recalls

### [official-rule] 450 - A Recall is when a Permanent is relocated from anywhere to its Base without it being a...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 450
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Recall is when a Permanent is relocated from anywhere to its Base without it being a Move.

### [official-rule] 451 - Recalls are not Moves.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 451
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Recalls are not Moves.

### [official-rule] 451.1 - They do not cause Triggered Abilities to trigger that are triggered by Move actions.

- parent_rule: 451
- section_id: 451
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> They do not cause Triggered Abilities to trigger that are triggered by Move actions.

### [official-rule] 451.2 - A Recall causes a Permanent to change locations.

- parent_rule: 451
- section_id: 451
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Recall causes a Permanent to change locations.

### [official-rule] 451.3 - A Recall cannot be prevented by actions and Game Effects that restrict or block Movement.

- parent_rule: 451
- section_id: 451
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Recall cannot be prevented by actions and Game Effects that restrict or block Movement.

### [official-rule] 452 - Gear can be Recalled.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 452
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Gear can be Recalled.

### [official-rule] 452.1 - When an un-attached Gear is created or played at a battlefield, or is at a battlefield...

- parent_rule: 452
- section_id: 452
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When an un-attached Gear is created or played at a battlefield, or is at a battlefield for any other reason, it is Recalled to its controller's base during the next Cleanup. Example: An Equipment is attached to a unit at a battlefield, so the Equipment is present at that battlefield. If the unit dies, the Equipment will be recalled during the next cleanup.

### [official-rule] 453 - Recalls do not affect the state of the Permanent being recalled.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 453
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Recalls do not affect the state of the Permanent being recalled.

### [official-rule] 453.1 - Unless otherwise stated by the source of the Recall, Damage, Exhausted Status, Buffed S...

- parent_rule: 453
- section_id: 453
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Unless otherwise stated by the source of the Recall, Damage, Exhausted Status, Buffed Status, and applied Layer alterations will all remain unaffected by a Recall.

### [official-rule] 454 - Combat

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 454
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Combat

### [official-rule] 455 - A Combat occurs when a Cleanup occurs, there are no items on the Chain, there is a stag...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 455
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A Combat occurs when a Cleanup occurs, there are no items on the Chain, there is a staged Combat at a Battlefield, and no Showdown or Combat is ongoing at any other Battlefield. See rule 318. Cleanups for more information.

### [official-rule] 455.1 - If there is an ongoing Showdown at the Battlefield where the Combat is staged, that Sho...

- parent_rule: 455
- section_id: 455
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If there is an ongoing Showdown at the Battlefield where the Combat is staged, that Showdown will become a Combat Showdown and a Combat will be initiated there.

### [official-rule] 456 - Combat is considered Staged if there are units controlled by two opposing players at a...

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 456
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Combat is considered Staged if there are units controlled by two opposing players at a Battlefield but the Steps of Combat have not been initiated.

### [official-rule] 456.1 - If more than one Battlefield has Units controlled by opposing players at it at the same...

- parent_rule: 456
- section_id: 456
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If more than one Battlefield has Units controlled by opposing players at it at the same time, the Turn Player decides which Combat to resolve first.

### [official-rule] 456.2 - If Staged Combats stop being Staged before the Steps of Combat are initiated, they are...

- parent_rule: 456
- section_id: 456
- page: 68
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If Staged Combats stop being Staged before the Steps of Combat are initiated, they are not resolved or executed.

### [official-rule] 456.3 - If a Combat and Showdown are staged at the same Battlefield and the turn player initiat...

- parent_rule: 456
- section_id: 456
- page: 68-69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Combat and Showdown are staged at the same Battlefield and the turn player initiates the Showdown, it will open as a Combat Showdown.

### [official-rule] 457 - Combat can only occur between Units controlled by exactly two players.

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 457
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Combat can only occur between Units controlled by exactly two players.

### [official-rule] 457.1 - In Modes of Play with more than two players, Battlefields with Staged Combats or Combat...

- parent_rule: 457
- section_id: 457
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In Modes of Play with more than two players, Battlefields with Staged Combats or Combats in Progress are Invalid Destinations for Moves of all kinds (Standard Moves or otherwise) by Units controlled by Players not involved in those Combats or who don't already have Units at that Battlefield. See rule 442.2.a. for more information on Invalid Destinations.

### [official-rule] 457.2 - In Modes of Play with more than two players, Battlefields with Staged Combats or Combat...

- parent_rule: 457
- section_id: 457
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> In Modes of Play with more than two players, Battlefields with Staged Combats or Combats in Progress are Invalid to be chosen as a location to play one or more Units by a player not involved in that Combat by any means,

### [official-rule] 457.2.a - If an effect would require a Unit be played to a Battlefield with a Staged Combat or a...

- parent_rule: 457.2
- section_id: 457
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If an effect would require a Unit be played to a Battlefield with a Staged Combat or a Combat in Progress, where the controller of the played unit is not a participant, instead the Unit is played to its controller's Base.

### [official-rule] 457.2.b - Any subsequent reference to "here" in the corresponding effect is reassigned to the Con...

- parent_rule: 457.2
- section_id: 457
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Any subsequent reference to "here" in the corresponding effect is reassigned to the Controller's Base, where the Unit was played. Any further effects that may be invalidated are invalidated as if the effect was mistargeted. See rule 355.6. Targeting for more information on Mistargeting.

### [official-rule] 457.3 - All choices that would result in a Combat occurring between more than two players simul...

- parent_rule: 457
- section_id: 457
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> All choices that would result in a Combat occurring between more than two players simultaneously are invalid and ineligible to be completed.

### [official-rule] 458 - The Steps of Combat

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 458
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Steps of Combat

### [official-rule] 459 - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

### [official-rule] 459.1 - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Combat will open in one of two ways: when a Combat and Showdown are staged at the same Battlefield and the turn player initiates the Showdown; or when the turn is in a Showdown Open State and a Combat is staged at the Battlefield where the current Showdown is ongoing. See rule 318. Cleanups for more information.

### [official-rule] 459.2 - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When Combat opens, it either opens with a Combat Showdown, or the current Showdown becomes a Combat Showdown.

### [official-rule] 459.2.a - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The following Tasks become Outstanding, in the order described:

### [official-rule] 459.2.b - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 1. Establish who is Attacker and who is Defender.

### [official-rule] 459.2.b.1 - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2.b
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Attacker is the player whose unit(s) applied the Contested status to the Battlefield. They gain the Attacker designation now.

### [official-rule] 459.2.b.1.a - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2.b.1
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> This player gains Focus as the showdown begins.

### [official-rule] 459.2.b.2 - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2.b
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Defender is the player who did not apply the Contested status to the Battlefield. They gain the Defender designation now.

### [official-rule] 459.2.b.3 - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2.b
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units at the Contested Battlefield controlled by the Attacker gain the Attacker designation now.

### [official-rule] 459.2.b.3.a - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2.b.3
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Unit controlled by the Attacker becomes present at this Battlefield after this moment, it will gain the Attacker designation during the Cleanup phase following the action that caused it to become present

### [official-rule] 459.2.b.4 - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2.b
- section_id: 459
- page: 69
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units at the Contested Battlefield controlled by the Defender gain the Defender designation now.

### [official-rule] 459.2.b.4.a - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2.b.4
- section_id: 459
- page: 69-70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Unit controlled by the Defender becomes present at this Battlefield after this moment it will gain the Defender designation during the Cleanup phase following the action that caused it to become present

### [official-rule] 459.2.c - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2
- section_id: 459
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 2. The Attacker gains Focus.

### [official-rule] 459.2.d - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2
- section_id: 459
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 3. Add items to the Combat Chain if establishing Attacker and Defender has caused Triggered Abilities to become Pending.

### [official-rule] 459.2.d.1 - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2.d
- section_id: 459
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The Attacking player, who has Focus, places Triggered Abilities on the Chain first, followed by all non-Defender players in Turn Order, followed by the Defending Player.

### [official-rule] 459.2.e - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2
- section_id: 459
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> The State Closes if a Combat Chain was created.

### [official-rule] 459.2.e.1 - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2.e
- section_id: 459
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Otherwise the Combat Showdown continues, with the State Open as normal.

### [official-rule] 459.2.f - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2
- section_id: 459
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Players proceed with any play on the Chain as normal.

### [official-rule] 459.2.g - Step 1: The Combat Showdown Step

- parent_rule: 459.2
- section_id: 459
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Focus does not pass upon closure of the Combat Chain, if any.

### [official-rule] 460 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

### [official-rule] 460.1 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If both Attacking and Defending units remain at this battlefield, the following Tasks become Outstanding, in the specified order:

### [official-rule] 460.2 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 1. When the Showdown closes, Attackers and Defenders resolve Combat Damage at the Battlefield that was attacked, using their current Might.

### [official-rule] 460.2.a - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Sum the Might of all Attacking Units.

### [official-rule] 460.2.b - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Sum the Might of all Defending Units.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Starting with the Attacker, each player assigns an amount of damage equal to their summed Might among the other's Units.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c.1 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2.c
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Assigning Damage is not Dealing Damage.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c.1.a - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2.c.1
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> When all Damage is assigned, it will be Dealt simultaneously. These actions are not synonymous.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c.2 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2.c
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Abilities or effects may influence the order in which damage is assigned. Reminder: Lethal Damage is non-zero damage equaling or exceeding the Might of a Unit.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c.3 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2.c
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units must have lethal damage assigned to them in full before damage is assigned to a different Unit. Example: If a player has 5 damage to distribute among four 3 Might units, they may not choose to assign 2 damage to one of the units and 1 damage to each of the remaining 3. They must assign at least 3 damage to one, and the remaining 2 to another.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c.4 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2.c
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Units cannot have more damage assigned to them than the minimum required to constitute lethal damage unless no further units remain to have damage assigned to them.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c.5 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2.c
- section_id: 460
- page: 70
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> A player must obey all requirements and restrictions on damage assignment if able. Example: A player is assigning damage to the following units: a unit with Tank ("I must be assigned combat damage first."); Caitlyn, Patrolling ("I must be assigned combat damage last."); and another unit without any abilities. That player must assign combat damage first to the unit with Tank, then to the unit with no abilities, then to Caitlyn.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c.6 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2.c
- section_id: 460
- page: 71
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If multiple Units have abilities or effects that require a player to assign them damage with the same priority, that player may assign damage to those units in any order. Example: A player is assigning damage to the following units: two units with Tank ("I must be assigned combat damage first.") and one unit with no abilities. That player chooses one of the units with Tank and assigns combat damage to it. Then they must assign any remaining damage first to the other unit with Tank, then to the unit with no abilities.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c.7 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2.c
- section_id: 460
- page: 71
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a Unit has one or more Abilities or effects applying to it that demand it be assigned damage in a specific way that is exclusionary, then the assigning player chooses only one of those abilities to apply when assigning damage. Example: Caitlyn, Patrolling ("I must be assigned combat damage last.") has been given the Tank ability ("I must be assigned combat damage first."). A player is assigning damage to this Caitlyn with Tank and two units with no abilities. That player can’t fulfill both of Caitlyn’s damage requirements, so they may choose to assign damage to Caitlyn first, fulfilling the Tank requirement, or last, fulfilling Caitlyn’s printed requirement. They can’t choose to apply damage to Caitlyn in between the other two units, because that wouldn’t fulfill either requirement.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c.8 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2.c
- section_id: 460
- page: 71
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If there is more than one unit in which this situation applies to, each unit is dealt with individually. The assigning player chooses which ability or effect applies, and then resolves the assignment. If this creates a situation where now more than one unit must be assigned with the same priority, those units may be assigned damage in any order as normal within that priority. Example: Two copies of Caitlyn, Patrolling ("I must be assigned combat damage last.") have been given the Tank ability ("I must be assigned combat damage first."). A player assigning damage to these two Caitlyns and one unit with no abilities could choose to fulfill both Caitlyns’ Tank requirements by assigning them both damage before the other unit.

### [official-rule] 460.2.c.9 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2.c
- section_id: 460
- page: 71
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> If a unit cannot be dealt damage, then no amount of damage can be considered lethal. Such a unit is exempt from any considerations of mandatory assignment. Example: Kayn, Unleashed says “If I have moved twice this turn, I don't take damage.” While Kayn can’t take damage, it is ignored for the purposes of assigning lethal damage in combat.

### [official-rule] 460.2.d - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460.2
- section_id: 460
- page: 71
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> Deal Damage to each unit equal to the amount assigned to it.

### [official-rule] 460.3 - Step 2: The Combat Damage Step

- parent_rule: 460
- section_id: 460
- page: 71
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf

Official rule text:

> 2. Skip the FEPR process and proceed to the Resolution Step.

### [official-rule] 461 - 4 The following Task becomes Outstanding:

- parent_rule: None
- section_id: 461
- page: 71
- source: core-rules-20260330.pdf
